# 84 — Testing

This file specifies how DocChat is tested: the test levels and where each lives and runs; the local environment (the `compose.yaml` services, the OpenAI-compatible model stub, the fixtures, the shared test harnesses and the event-loop guard); the generated authorisation matrix test; the cross-project and cross-user isolation suite; the catalogue that gives every required test its identifier (`T-nnn`), its level and what it proves; the deployed suites and the test identities they use; the load test with its machine-checked pass criteria; the infrastructure checks; the security scans and their thresholds; and the coverage and gating rules of CI. Other files name tests by their descriptive names; section 5 gives each of those names its identifier. Test identifiers never change once published.

## 1. Strategy and test levels

### 1.1 Principles

1. **Nothing outside three levels needs Azure.** Unit, integration, contract, browser, migration and infrastructure tests run on a developer machine or a GitHub-hosted runner with no Azure credential. Only the live, deployed and load levels reach Azure, each behind its own explicit flag or workflow.
2. **Only the model is stubbed by a service.** The OpenAI-compatible model stub (section 2.3) is a separate process that an app reaches only because its endpoint setting points at it. Production code holds no reference to it, no switch selects it, and the import contracts of `01-repository.md` section 2.4 keep `docchat_testing` and `docchat_model_stub` out of every production package.
3. **Services without an emulator are replaced through the composition root.** Azure AI Search, Content Safety Prompt Shields and Document Intelligence are reached in tests through recorded HTTP exchanges or in-memory fakes passed to `create_app(ports=…)` or the ingestion activity context, never through a setting (section 2.4). Each fake has a contract suite that also runs against the real `dev` service.
4. **Time and randomness are injected.** Leases, deadlines, TTL arithmetic, backoff and cache lifetimes are tested through `docchat_testing.clock.FakeClock` passed to each component's clock port; no test sleeps to wait for a deadline.
5. **Every content value is a canary.** Questions, answers, chunks, file names, facts, e-mail addresses, display names, instructions, comments, titles and summaries in tests come from `builders.canary(kind)`, and the session-wide audit of section 2.10 fails the session when one reaches telemetry.
6. **Every test runs under the event-loop guard.** asyncio debug mode is on for every test, and any callback that holds the loop for more than 100 ms fails the test (section 2.8).
7. **Limiters are permissive unless they are the subject.** Every app built by the test fixtures receives the permissive burst and write limiters, so suites that send hundreds of requests from one user never meet a `429`; a test whose subject is a limiter's answer asks for the real limiters with `real_limiters=True`. The authorisation matrix and the isolation suite always run with the permissive limiters. No setting disables a limiter.
8. **One home per requirement.** Each required test has one identifier in section 5 and one level; a requirement proven at two levels has two identifiers.

### 1.2 Levels

| Level | Location | Runs against | Tool | When it runs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unit (Python) | `<member>/tests/unit/`, mirroring `src/` module for module | the process only: `httpx` mocked with respx; Azure SDK clients given `ReplayTransport`; ports without emulators given in-memory fakes; time through `FakeClock` | pytest | every CI run (`python-tests` job) |
| Unit and component (web) | `apps/web/src/**/*.test.ts(x)`, beside each module | jsdom; MSW with `onUnhandledRequest: "error"`; Vitest fake timers where a test names them | Vitest 5.0.4 with Testing Library | every CI run (`web` job) |
| Integration (per member) | `<member>/tests/integration/` | the `compose.yaml` services: SQL Server, Azurite, the Cosmos DB emulator, the model stub | pytest, `integration` marker set automatically | every CI run |
| Cross-service integration | `tests/integration/` (`isolation/`, `contracts/`, `chat/`, `ingestion/`, `ops/`, `data_access/`) | the API in process (ASGI transport, or Uvicorn on `127.0.0.1` with a random port for streaming and disconnects), the ingestion orchestrators and activities through the Durable harness, the compose services | pytest | every CI run |
| Contract | `tests/integration/contracts/` | each in-memory fake on every CI run, and the matching `dev` service with `--live` | pytest | every CI run (fake half); with the live level (service half) |
| Live | `<member>/tests/live/` and the live half of the contract suites | the `dev` environment's Azure services | `uv run pytest -m live --live` | by an operator from a machine in the `dev` operator allowlist, at each stage acceptance and before a release that changes an adapter |
| Deployed | `tests/deployed/` | a deployed environment's public endpoints with test-user tokens; the infrastructure suite with the `dev` deploy identity | `uv run pytest tests/deployed --deployed -m <suite>` | section 6.5 |
| Browser | `tests/e2e/specs/web/` (stub API and stub identity provider) and `tests/e2e/specs/web-deployed/` (unauthenticated checks of a deployed site) | the production web build under the Static Web Apps CLI emulator; the deployed site | Playwright 1.64.1 with `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 and `@azure/static-web-apps-cli` 2.0.10 | every pull request (`e2e` job); after every web deploy (`web-deployed` job) |
| Load | `tests/load/` and `docchat_testing.load` | the short-lived `load` environment | k6 2.3.0 (scenarios S1 to S3), the Python driver `docchat-load ingest` (S4) | section 7.9 |
| Infrastructure | `infra/`, `ps-rule.yaml`, `.ps-rule/` | Bicep build and lint, PSRule, what-if against `dev` | Azure CLI with Bicep CLI 0.47.16, PSRule | every pull request (section 8) |
| Migration | `db/` | the SQL Server container | Alembic | every CI run (`migrations` job, section 10.3) |
| Manual | `docs/runbooks/rehearsals.md` records | a deployed environment, by a named person | the runbook it belongs to | at the release or stage the item names |

Every version above was confirmed on 2026-10-14 and is pinned in `01-repository.md` section 5 (versions table). The pytest configuration (markers `integration`, `live`, `deployed`, `smoke`, `full`, `resume`, `prod_smoke`, `scan_prepare`, `scan`, `infra` and `allow_blocking(reason)`; warnings as errors; strict markers; a 60-second default timeout) is `01-repository.md` section 5.4's; this file adds the per-test timeouts: deployed tests 900 s each, the 50 MB upload test 1,800 s, the start of the ingestion resume test 3,600 s.

### 1.3 Where each kind of test goes

1. A module's own behaviour: a unit test in its member's `tests/unit/`, mirroring the module path (`src/<pkg>/<path>/<name>.py` → `tests/unit/<path>/test_<name>.py`). The layout check (section 10.2) fails a module without its test file unless it is listed as exempt with a reason.
2. Behaviour that needs SQL Server, Azurite, the Cosmos DB emulator or the model stub inside one member: that member's `tests/integration/`.
3. Behaviour that crosses the API and ingestion, or a suite that spans every section (the isolation suite, the contract suites, the data-access audits): `tests/integration/`.
4. The authorisation matrix: `apps/api/tests/integration/test_authorization_matrix.py`.
5. Web modules and components: beside the module in `apps/web/src`; browser flows in `tests/e2e/specs/web/`.
6. Anything that needs a deployed environment: `tests/deployed/` (Python) or `tests/e2e/specs/web-deployed/` (Playwright), never inside a member.
7. The test-support package's own tests (meta-tests of the guard, the audits and the generators): `tests/support/tests/`, run with pytester where a test must observe another test failing.

### 1.4 The test-support package `docchat_testing`

`tests/support` is the workspace member `docchat-testing` (`01-repository.md` section 1.8). No production image contains it. Its modules:

| Module | Provides |
|---|---|
| `plugin.py` | the pytest options `--live`, `--deployed` and `--stage-acceptance` (without it the stage-acceptance tests of `tests/deployed` skip, section 6.5); the option `--compiled` (the directory of compiled templates) is defined by `tests/infra/conftest.py`, not here; automatic `integration` marking of every test under a `tests/integration/` directory; compose readiness checks that fail the session (never skip) when a selected integration test meets an unreachable service; the loop guard (section 2.8); the telemetry capture and canary audit (section 2.10) |
| `fixtures/sql.py` | one database `docchat_test_<8 hex>` per session on the SQL container, migrated to head once; after each test one batch of `DELETE` statements empties every `app` table in reverse foreign-key order and re-inserts the ops-config seed rows exactly as the migrations wrote them; one SQL login per database role (`docchat_api`, `docchat_ingestion`, `docchat_config_operator`, `docchat_conversation_reader`, `docchat_user_data_operator`) so grant tests run as each role |
| `fixtures/cosmos.py` | one database per session holding the three production containers `conversations`, `memories` and `usage` with their partition keys, TTL and indexing policies (`80-data-model.md` section 3), deleted at session end; tests isolate themselves with fresh random user IDs |
| `fixtures/storage.py` | Azurite through `UseDevelopmentStorage=true`; queue names suffixed with the session's 8-hex ID; blob paths isolated by fresh IDs |
| `fixtures/api.py` | `create_app(settings=…, ports=…, signing_keys=…)`; an `httpx.AsyncClient` with `ASGITransport` that runs the app's lifespan through `app.router.lifespan_context(app)`; `live_api_server` (Uvicorn in the test's own loop on a random port) for streaming and disconnect tests; `ports` carries `fakes.PermissiveBurstLimiter` and `fakes.PermissiveWriteRateLimiter` (each allows every request and records it) unless the test passes `real_limiters=True` |
| `fixtures/identities.py` | the five shared principals of section 2.5 |
| `tokens.py` | a per-session RS256 2,048-bit key pair and its key set, served to the API's injectable signing-key provider; `mint_token(principal, **claim_overrides)` producing the v2 access tokens the API validates (`11-api.md` section 1.2); `load_passwords(vault_uri, aliases)` and `acquire_ropc_token(alias)` for deployed and load tests (section 6.4) |
| `apiclient.py` | `DeployedApiClient`, the one HTTP client of the deployed suites and `docchat-load` (section 6.4 step 7) |
| `resume_check.py` | `python -m docchat_testing.resume_check`, the `dev` half of the deployed crash-resume test (section 6.7) |
| `logquery.py` | `query_logs(workspace_id, kql, timespan)`, the Log Analytics query API client (`httpx.AsyncClient`, timeout 30 s, `follow_redirects=False`, token for `https://api.loganalytics.io/.default` from `azure.identity.aio.AzureCliCredential`); `python -m docchat_testing.logquery chat-telemetry --workspace-id <id> --since <UTC time>`, the person-run check T-282 (section 6.3) |
| `builders/` | one builder per table and Cosmos DB document type with valid defaults, and `canary(kind)` |
| `fakes/` | `FakeSearchGateway`, `FakePromptShields` and `FakeLayoutAnalyzer`, each implementing its owning component's port; `PermissiveBurstLimiter` and `PermissiveWriteRateLimiter`; `ReplayTransport`, an azure-core `AsyncHttpTransport` serving JSON exchanges from `tests/fixtures/http/<service>/` |
| `clock.py` | `FakeClock` with `now()`, `monotonic()` and `advance(seconds)` |
| `durable.py` | `OrchestrationHarness` (section 2.12) |
| `loopguard.py` | the `blocked_loop_guard` fixture (section 2.8) |
| `telemetry_capture.py` | in-memory span, log and metric exporters and the session audit (section 2.10) |
| `authz_matrix.py` | the generator of the authorisation matrix test (section 3) |
| `isolation.py` | the registry `DATA_PATHS` and the runner of the isolation suite (section 4) |
| `data_audit.py` | `CosmosAudit`, `CosmosFaults`, the `@request_path` registry and the SQL plan audit (section 2.11) |
| `streaming.py` | `collect_stream(response)` (section 2.9) |
| `load/` | the `docchat-load` console script: commands `tokens`, `seed`, `aci-params`, `ingest`, `build-corpus`, `s3-rate`, `verdict`, `probe-alerts` (section 7) |

Every outbound client in the package follows `01-repository.md` section 6.4: explicit timeouts (Key Vault: connection 5 s, read 30 s; Log Analytics and Graph: 30 s; GitHub OIDC token: 10 s), `follow_redirects=False` on every `httpx.AsyncClient`.

### 1.5 Fixture documents, questions and streams

`tests/fixtures/documents/` holds the four documents every level shares, each under 1 MB, with synthetic text written for this project:

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `tables.pdf` | 12 pages, 4 tables, one spanning two pages, one with merged header cells |
| `scanned.pdf` | 6 image-only pages at 200 dpi, no text layer |
| `headings.docx` | three heading levels, 18 sections, 2 tables |
| `slides.pptx` | 10 slides, 2 with tables, speaker notes on 3 |

Beside each sits `<file>.expected.json` (the expected chunks per method, `14-ingestion.md` section 10) and the recorded layout result the Document Intelligence fake returns: the `AnalyzeResult` JSON of `prebuilt-layout` (API version `2024-11-30`, Markdown output) recorded with `stringIndexType=unicodeCodePoint`, so its offsets and `pages[].spans` count code points; each expectation's page numbers follow the page rule of `14-ingestion.md` section 10. `generate.py` beside them produced the files; they change only through a reviewed regeneration. The archive and document-check fixtures (`bomb.docx`, `many-entries.pptx`, `macro.docm-as.docx`, `encrypted.pdf`, `blank-scan.pdf`, `external-template.docx`, `dde-field.docx`, `remote-image.pptx`, `ole-package.docx`, `active-content.pdf`) are `14-ingestion.md` sections 14 and 15.6's and live beside these; the four DOCX and PPTX among them that hold a remote template, a DDE field, a linked image or an embedded package pass `inspect` and meet the converter. The converter fixtures of `14-ingestion.md` section 15.11 live beside them too, each written by `generate.py`: `include-fields.docx` (an `INCLUDETEXT` and an `INCLUDEPICTURE` field, each naming `file:///etc/hostname` and a canary file in a sibling scratch directory of another parse user), `linked-section.docx`, `linked-image-unc.docx`, `linked-image-http.pptx`, `ole-link.docx`, `ole-link.pptx`, `xxe.docx` (a part declaring an external entity that names `file:///etc/hostname`), `vba-autoopen.docx` and `vba-autoopen.pptx` (a document-type package carrying a `vbaProject.bin` whose `AutoOpen` or `Auto_Open` macro writes a marker file; `inspect` refuses both with `macro_content_declared`, rule `macro.vba`, and the converter tests convert them directly, so they prove the profile's macro settings as a second layer). So do the passing base files, which `generate.py` also writes: `base.docx` (one heading, one paragraph, one `https` hyperlink, one header part, and one embedded `.xlsx` shown as an OLE object), `base.pptx` (two slides, one with speaker notes), `base-chart.pptx` (one slide with a chart whose data sits in an embedded `.xlsx`), `base.pdf` (one page of text) and `owner-only.pdf` (one page with an owner password and an empty user password); every fixture of the archive and document check unit tests is one of these with one change, written by `generate.py` beside it. Six passing controls saved by Office sit beside them, committed as Office saved them and never written by `generate.py`: the content of `base.docx`, `base.pptx` and `base-chart.pptx` built in current Word and PowerPoint and saved once in the Transitional form (`office-base.docx`, `office-base.pptx`, `office-base-chart.pptx`) and once in the Strict form (`office-base-strict.docx`, `office-base-strict.pptx`, `office-base-chart-strict.pptx`), each with a chart and with page or slide numbers, the Word files with a table of contents (`TOC`, `PAGEREF` and `HYPERLINK \l` fields) and `PAGE` and `NUMPAGES` fields in a footer too. `office-base.docx` and `office-base-strict.docx` each contain one comment with one reply, saved by current Word. Each control passes `inspect` and converts to a PDF. The pull request that adds or replaces one names the Office version and build that saved it. The rendition schema fixtures of `14-ingestion.md` section 14.8, written by `generate.py` beside `active-content.pdf`, each one page of text with one construct the schema leaves out: `schema-presteps-gotor.pdf` (a page `/PresSteps` whose navigation node's `/NA` is a `/GoToR` action with no `/Type`), `schema-gotoe-unlisted.pdf` (a `/GoToE` action under an unlisted page key `/X1`), `schema-page-af.pdf` (a page `/AF` array holding a file specification with `/EF` and no `/Type`), `schema-image-alternates.pdf` (an image XObject with `/Alternates`), `schema-form-ref.pdf` (a form XObject with `/Ref` naming page 1 of `other.pdf`), `schema-stream-f.pdf` (an image stream whose `/F` names `x.bin`), `schema-font-unlisted.pdf` (a font dictionary with an unlisted key `/X1` holding a `/URI` action), `schema-annotation-ap.pdf` (a `Square` annotation and a filled form field, each with a normal appearance stream, and a `Link` to the same page) and `schema-resources-unknown.pdf` (an unknown dictionary `/X1 << /S /Launch /F (calc.exe) >>` under `/Resources`). The schema limit fixtures, written the same way: `schema-depth-65.pdf` (form XObjects nested so that one path runs 65 containers below the page), `schema-containers-100001.pdf` (one page of 100,001 distinct containers) and the generated document of the schema limits test (2,000 pages whose containers total 1,000,001). The inline dictionary fixtures of `14-ingestion.md` section 15.6 item 14.2, invariant 5, written the same way, each one page whose content stream the schema copies as written: `schema-inline-af.pdf` (`/Span << /AF [ << /Type /Filespec /F (a.pdf) >> ] >> BDC` … `EMC` in the page content), `schema-inline-image-url.pdf` (an inline image `BI /W 1 /H 1 /BPC 8 /CS /G /URL (https://example.net/x) ID` … `EI`) and the passing control `schema-inline-tagged.pdf` (`BDC` property lists with `/MCID`, `/ActualText`, `/Alt`, `/Lang` and `/E`, and an optional-content `/OC /oc1 BDC` naming a `/Properties` resource). The whole-document fixture `schema-memo-two-pages.pdf`, written the same way: two pages laid out alike, each drawing its own text in its own embedded font and its own image, so that `render` writes both page files with their font and image under the same object numbers.

`tests/fixtures/questions/load.json` holds the load questions (synthetic, answerable from the four fixture documents); `tests/fixtures/questions/jailbreak.json` the jailbreak question of the deployed content-filter test; `tests/fixtures/documents/indirect-attack.pdf` the indirect-attack document of the deployed screening test.

`tests/fixtures/streams/*.ndjson` holds recorded chat streams, including 2-, 3- and 4-byte UTF-8 characters and a 70 KB line; the API's stream tests and the web client's reader tests read the same files (`10-web-client.md` section 7).

`tests/fixtures/http/<service>/` holds the recorded exchanges `ReplayTransport` and respx serve, for the exact API versions in use: AI Search `2026-04-01`, Content Safety `2024-09-01`, Document Intelligence `2024-11-30`.

**The load corpus.** `docchat-load build-corpus --variant <a|b> --count 200 --out <dir>` writes 100 copies of `tables.pdf`, 40 of `scanned.pdf`, 40 of `headings.docx` and 20 of `slides.pptx`, each made byte-unique by appending the PDF comment line `%docchat-load-<variant>-<nnn>` after `%%EOF` (readers ignore it) or by setting the ZIP archive comment to the same text (DOCX, PPTX), named `load-<variant>-<nnn>.<ext>`. The SHA-256 duplicate check therefore sees 200 new documents while parse cost stays representative and repeatable.

### 1.6 Live tests

Marker `live`, run only with `uv run pytest -m live --live`, from a machine whose public address is in the `dev` operator allowlist, signed in with `az login` as an operator holding the just-in-time data roles (`86-operations.md`); endpoints from `.azure/dev/.env`. Contents: the live half of the contract suites (section 2.4); the SQL Entra-token connection through the data-access layer; blob soft delete, versioning and index tags.

The live contract suites run in `dev` only, by a member of `sg-docchat-dev-config-operators` (just-in-time level `config`), under the group's bootstrap assignments at `rg-docchat-dev` (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 6.3 and 6.9). Cognitive Services User (`a97b65f3-24c7-4388-baec-2e87135dc908`) covers the Prompt Shields and Document Intelligence suites. The AI Search suite writes and deletes chunks, so it works in an index of its own under Search Service Contributor (`7ca78c08-252a-4471-8644-bb5ff32d4ba0`, index create and delete) and Search Index Data Contributor (`8ebe5a00-799e-43f5-93ac-243d3dce84a7`, document upload, delete and query):

1. Before it creates anything, the suite lists the service's indexes and fails when they number more than 13, so Basic's 15-index limit keeps a slot free for a rebuild; it then deletes every index named `contract-<UTC timestamp>-<8 hex>` whose timestamp is more than 2 hours old, the leftover of a run ended before its clean-up.
2. It creates `contract-<yyyymmddhhmmss>-<8 random hex>` (UTC; 32 characters) with the physical index schema of `80-data-model.md` section 6. No application index uses the `contract-` prefix: every physical index name matches `^docchat-[0-9a-f]{32}-g[1-9][0-9]{0,4}$` (`80-data-model.md` section 6.2).
3. It runs its cases against that index and deletes the index in a `finally` block; a failed delete fails the run with the index name printed, and step 1 of a later run removes it. Prompt Shields' handling of an empty `userPrompt` is probed by `docchat-ops --env dev chat model-check` (`12-chat-pipeline.md` section 10.7), not by a live test. Live tests never run in CI, because GitHub-hosted runners can't reach private endpoints.

## 2. Local environment

### 2.1 `compose.yaml`

Project name `docchat`; every host port bound to `127.0.0.1`; named volumes `sqlserver-data`, `azurite-data`, `cosmos-data`. Each third-party image is pinned by the full digest of its tag as published on 2026-10-14 (the table shows the digest's first and last characters for identification); Dependabot moves a digest, and a newer Cosmos DB emulator tag is adopted only through a Dependabot pull request on which the full integration suite passes.

| Service | Image or build | Ports (host → container) | Settings | Readiness |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sqlserver` | `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2025-CU9-GDR1-ubuntu-24.04@sha256:742481d1…ffbab2`, `platform: linux/amd64` | 14330 → 1433 | `ACCEPT_EULA=Y`, `MSSQL_PID=Developer`, `MSSQL_SA_PASSWORD=${DOCCHAT_LOCAL_SQL_SA_PASSWORD:?run docchat-ops local init}` | healthcheck `/opt/mssql-tools18/bin/sqlcmd -C -S localhost -U sa -P "$$MSSQL_SA_PASSWORD" -Q "SELECT 1" -b -o /dev/null`, every 6 s, timeout 5 s, 20 retries, start period 30 s |
| `azurite` | `mcr.microsoft.com/azure-storage/azurite:3.37.0@sha256:830430c1…71fd5` | 10000, 10001, 10002 | command `azurite --blobHost 0.0.0.0 --queueHost 0.0.0.0 --tableHost 0.0.0.0 --location /data --skipApiVersionCheck --loose` | healthcheck `node -e` TCP connect to `127.0.0.1:10000`, every 5 s, 12 retries |
| `cosmos` | `mcr.microsoft.com/cosmosdb/linux/azure-cosmos-emulator:vnext-EN20260907@sha256:2db1f9e7…ec02` (the first tag with working hierarchical partition keys) | 8081 → 8081 (gateway, HTTP), 18080 → 8080 (health probe) | `PROTOCOL=http`, `ENABLE_TELEMETRY=false` | the test plugin polls `http://127.0.0.1:18080/ready` for up to 180 s |
| `model-stub` | build `tests/model-stub/Dockerfile`, tag `docchat/model-stub:local` | 8091 → 8000 | the defaults of section 2.3, `DOCCHAT_STUB_RECORD=true`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_WORKERS=1` | healthcheck `python -c` GET `http://127.0.0.1:8000/healthz`, every 5 s, 12 retries |

`docchat-ops --env local local init` writes the gitignored `.env` with a generated 24-character `DOCCHAT_LOCAL_SQL_SA_PASSWORD`; CI generates a password per job. Commands: `docker compose up -d --wait` (local and CI) and `docker compose down -v` (CI cleanup, always run).

**Emulator limits the tests respect.** The Cosmos DB emulator has no request-unit accounting and no range or composite indexes, so no test depends on them; request-unit measurement happens in the load environment (section 7.7). Azurite lacks blob soft delete and versioning and has blob-index tags in preview; live tests cover the real behaviour. The SQL container has SQL authentication only; Entra logins are proven by the stage-0 SQL test (`90-build-order.md`) and the live tests.

### 2.2 Local-emulator mode and its guard

The app setting `DOCCHAT_LOCAL_EMULATORS` (bool, default `false`, set only in a local `.env`) switches the client factory and the SQL layer to the local services: SQL authentication with `DOCCHAT_LOCAL_SQL_SA_PASSWORD` against `127.0.0.1,14330` (`Encrypt=yes;TrustServerCertificate=yes`), `UseDevelopmentStorage=true` for Blob and Queue, `http://127.0.0.1:8081` with the emulator's published key for Cosmos DB, and `http://` model and screening endpoints on loopback. Start-up validation stops the process with exit code 78 and code `local_emulators_in_azure` when the flag is `true` and `WEBSITE_SITE_NAME` or `WEBSITE_INSTANCE_ID` is set, and with `insecure_endpoint` when the flag is `false` and any configured endpoint uses `http://` or a loopback host (`81-config.md` section 6). Tests construct clients directly and never set the flag.

### 2.3 The model stub

Package `docchat_model_stub` (FastAPI with the API's pins), image `docchat/model-stub`, served by `uvicorn docchat_model_stub.app:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --workers ${DOCCHAT_STUB_WORKERS}`. An app reaches it only through its endpoint setting: `DOCCHAT_OPENAI_ENDPOINT` locally, in CI and in `load` (where the stub runs on its own App Service site, `83-infrastructure.md` section 2.21), and `DOCCHAT_CONTENTSAFETY_ENDPOINT` in local and CI runs only; in `load` the Content Safety endpoint is the real account, so the load test measures real screening on the chat path.

**Model endpoints.** `POST /openai/v1/chat/completions` and `POST /openai/v1/embeddings`. Every request needs `Authorization: Bearer <any>` (`401` with an Azure-shaped error body otherwise); the header is never logged or stored; the `api-version` query is ignored.

**Parameter checks.** A chat request carrying `temperature`, `top_p`, `max_tokens`, `presence_penalty` or `frequency_penalty` gets `400` code `unsupported_parameter`, as the reasoning models in use do; `reasoning_effort`, when present, must be exactly one of `none`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, and any other value, `minimal` included, gets `400` code `unsupported_value`. A configuration the real deployments would refuse therefore fails in stub-backed tests too.

**Structured outputs.** For `response_format.type = "json_schema"` the content is the next scripted response for that schema name (`PUT /_stub/scripts/{name}`) when one is queued, otherwise one JSON object generated by `schema_fill.py`: every required property present; strings `"stub-<property>"` cut or padded to `maxLength` and `minLength`; numbers at `minimum` (else 0); booleans `false`; arrays with `max(minItems, 1)` items where `maxItems` allows; enums take their first value; `const` honoured.

**Plain text.** A non-streamed text call returns `DOCCHAT_STUB_TEXT_TOKENS` words from a constant word list after `DOCCHAT_STUB_JSON_DELAY_MS`.

**Streams.** Server-sent events in the OpenAI chunk format: a role chunk; then `DOCCHAT_STUB_ANSWER_TOKENS` words in chunks of `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKENS_PER_CHUNK` words, the first after `DOCCHAT_STUB_FIRST_TOKEN_DELAY_MS`, each next after `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKEN_DELAY_MS` × words per chunk; after the first sentence, citation markers for up to two distinct source IDs found in the request messages with `DOCCHAT_STUB_SOURCE_ID_PATTERN`; a final chunk with `finish_reason: "stop"`; with `stream_options.include_usage`, a last chunk with `choices: []` and `usage` (`prompt_tokens` = ceil(characters of all messages ÷ 4), `completion_tokens` = words sent, `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` 0, `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` 0); then `data: [DONE]`. The generator sleeps between chunks with `asyncio.sleep`, checks `await request.is_disconnected()` before each chunk, and on a disconnect increments the deployment's `clientDisconnected` counter and stops.

**Embeddings.** One vector per input, length `dimensions` (3,072 when absent; `400` outside 1 to 3,072), unit-normalised, from a PRNG seeded with the SHA-256 of the input text, so equal texts give equal vectors; `input` is a string or an array of 1 to 2,048 strings (`400` otherwise); usage estimated as above; each response after `DOCCHAT_STUB_EMBEDDING_DELAY_MS`.

**Prompt Shields (local and CI runs only).** `POST /contentsafety/text:shieldPrompt` (`api-version` ignored), body `{userPrompt, documents}`: `400` code `InvalidRequestBody` when `documents` has more than 5 entries or the texts exceed 10,000 characters in total (the service's limits for API version `2024-09-01`); otherwise, when the script named `shield_prompt` holds a queued response (`PUT /_stub/scripts/shield_prompt` with `{"responses": [{"userPromptAnalysis": {"attackDetected": true}, "documentsAnalysis": []} | {"status": 503, "error": {"code", "message"}}]}`), the next one is consumed and returned as it stands, the `status` form as that HTTP status with the Azure-shaped error body, before any directive applies; otherwise `200` `{"userPromptAnalysis": {"attackDetected": <bool>}, "documentsAnalysis": [{"attackDetected": <bool>}…]}`, where an entry is `true` only when its text contains `[[stub:shield=attack]]`.

**Directives.** A token `[[stub:<directive>]]` in the last `user` message (for screening: in `userPrompt` or a document) changes that request only, matched by `\[\[stub:([a-z_]+)(?:=([0-9a-z_]+))?(?:@(stream|json|text|embed|shield))?\]\]`:

| Directive | Effect |
|---|---|
| `status=<code>` | that status with the body `{"error": {"code": "<status>", "message": "stub directive"}}`; `429` adds `retry-after-ms: DOCCHAT_STUB_RETRY_AFTER_MS` and `retry-after` (that value in whole seconds, rounded up) |
| `filter=prompt` | `400` `{"error": {"code": "content_filter", "message": "stub filter", "innererror": {"code": "ResponsibleAIPolicyViolation"}}}` |
| `filter=completion` | the stream ends with `finish_reason: "content_filter"` |
| `cut=<n>` | the connection closes after n content chunks, with no final chunk |
| `no_usage` | no usage chunk |
| `tokens=<n>` | n words in place of `DOCCHAT_STUB_ANSWER_TOKENS` |
| `delay_ms=<n>` | n ms per token in place of `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKEN_DELAY_MS` |
| `first_ms=<n>` | n ms before a stream's first content chunk, or before a non-streamed response, in place of `DOCCHAT_STUB_FIRST_TOKEN_DELAY_MS` or `DOCCHAT_STUB_JSON_DELAY_MS` |
| `hang` | headers, then nothing until the client disconnects |
| `shield=attack` | marks a screening text as an attack (above) |

A directive followed by `@stream`, `@json`, `@text`, `@embed` or `@shield` applies only to that kind of request (a question reaches the rewrite, embedding and answer calls, so `[[stub:status=500@stream]]` fails only the answer); without a qualifier it applies to every request carrying it. The chat pipeline's defanging touches only `[[BEGIN` and `[[END` (`12-chat-pipeline.md` section 9), so directives reach the stub unchanged. Directives exist only in test inputs; an unknown directive gets `400` code `unknown_stub_directive`.

**Throttling.** When `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_EVERY_N` > 0, every N-th request (counted per worker process and deployment) to the deployments listed in `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_DEPLOYMENTS` (empty = all) gets `429` with the headers above.

**Inspection (tests only).** `GET /healthz` (`200 {"status":"ok"}`); `GET /_stub/stats` (per deployment: started, completed, throttled, clientDisconnected); `GET /_stub/requests?after=<seq>` and `DELETE /_stub/requests` (the recorder: the last 2,000 requests with sequence number, time, path, deployment and JSON body, kept only when `DOCCHAT_STUB_RECORD=true`); `PUT /_stub/scripts/{name}` and `DELETE /_stub/scripts`.

**Request handling order.** (1) `401` without a bearer header; (2) parse the body and collect the directives that apply to the request's kind (an unknown one → `400 unknown_stub_directive`; screening: the `InvalidRequestBody` limits); (3) record the request when recording is on; (4) throttle; (5) a queued `shield_prompt` response, else a `status=` directive; (6) parameter checks and `filter=prompt`; (7) build the output: a queued script, a schema fill, plain text, embeddings, a screening result or a stream.

**Settings** (environment of the stub process; an invalid value stops it before it serves):

| Setting | Type | Default | Range | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_WORKERS` | integer | 1 | 1 to 8 | Uvicorn workers; 1 locally and in CI so stats, scripts and recordings are exact |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_RECORD` | bool | `true` | | request recorder on |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_FIRST_TOKEN_DELAY_MS` | integer | 300 | 0 to 60,000 | delay before the first content chunk |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKEN_DELAY_MS` | integer | 10 | 0 to 5,000 | delay per word |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKENS_PER_CHUNK` | integer | 1 | 1 to 16 | words per content chunk |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_ANSWER_TOKENS` | integer | 60 | 1 to 4,000 | words per streamed answer |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_TEXT_TOKENS` | integer | 40 | 1 to 4,000 | words per plain-text answer |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_JSON_DELAY_MS` | integer | 20 | 0 to 60,000 | delay of non-streamed answers |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_EMBEDDING_DELAY_MS` | integer | 5 | 0 to 10,000 | delay of embedding answers |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_EVERY_N` | integer | 0 (off) | 0 to 1,000 | `429` injection period |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_DEPLOYMENTS` | comma list | empty (all) | deployment names | `429` injection scope |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_RETRY_AFTER_MS` | integer | 200 | 0 to 60,000 | `retry-after-ms` on injected `429` |
| `DOCCHAT_STUB_SOURCE_ID_PATTERN` | regex | `\[\[BEGIN SOURCE (S\d{1,2}) ` | a regex with one group | finds the pipeline's source IDs for `[S<n>]` markers |

The `load` values are in section 7.3.

### 2.4 Search, screening and layout in tests

1. **Adapter unit tests** replay hand-written HTTP exchanges for the exact API versions in use through `ReplayTransport` (SDK clients) or respx (`httpx`).
2. **The search stub of the chat pipeline's integration tests** is the real async `SearchClient` given `ReplayTransport`, which serves the recorded `docs/search` responses of `tests/fixtures/http/search/` keyed by index name and request body, so those tests exercise the request shape (`VectorizedQuery` fields, `select`, semantic configuration) the pipeline sends.
3. **Domain flows elsewhere** (ingestion, the isolation suite, the operator commands) use the in-memory fakes, injected through `create_app(ports=…)` and the ingestion activity context.
4. **Contract suites** in `tests/integration/contracts/` run against each fake on every CI run and against the `dev` service with `--live`. Every behaviour the code relies on is written into the contract before the fake implements it: a deleted chunk is not returned; filters by `documentId`; reranker scores on the 0 to 4 scale; Prompt Shields' 5-document and 10,000-character limits; layout Markdown with HTML tables.

### 2.5 Test principals

`fixtures/identities.py` defines the five principals every in-process suite shares; each test creates the memberships, mappings, sessions and facts it needs with builder calls, so suites can use different project layouts without changing the principals:

| Principal | Token | Default role in the seeded world |
|---|---|---|
| `alice` | `roles: ["User"]` | member of project A |
| `bob` | `roles: ["User"]` | member of project B |
| `carol` | `roles: ["Admin"]` | no memberships |
| `dave` | `roles: ["User"]` | no projects |
| `erin` | a valid token with no `roles` claim | none |

Tokens come from `mint_token`, signed with the session key pair, with the claims `11-api.md` section 1.2 requires; a claim override produces the validator's matching failure reason.

### 2.6 Tests on the stub and the emulators

The stub's own conformance with the `openai` 3.31.0 `AsyncOpenAI` client, its directives, screening, throttling, recorder and parameter checks, and the emulator's hierarchical partition keys are proven by catalogue entries in section 5.13.

### 2.7 pytest and asyncio configuration

`asyncio_mode = "auto"`, function-scoped loops for fixtures and tests, `asyncio_debug = true`, `filterwarnings = ["error"]` (so "coroutine was never awaited" fails), `xfail_strict = true` (`01-repository.md` section 5.4). Fixture files are read in synchronous fixtures, never inside coroutines (Ruff `ASYNC230`).

### 2.8 The event-loop guard

With asyncio debug mode on, the autouse fixture `blocked_loop_guard`:

1. at setup, for any test that runs on an event loop, sets `loop.slow_callback_duration = 0.1` on the running loop;
2. attaches a handler to the `asyncio` logger that captures each `WARNING` whose message starts with `Executing ` and contains ` took ` (the debug-mode slow-callback record), and each record containing `Task was destroyed but it is pending` or `Task exception was never retrieved`;
3. at teardown removes the handler and fails the test with `Event loop blocked > 100 ms: <callback> took <seconds> s` (or the task message), listing every occurrence.

The in-process Uvicorn server of streaming tests runs on the test's own loop, so server code is covered. The only exemption is `@pytest.mark.allow_blocking(reason=…)`, which exempts one test and is allowed only in `tests/support/tests`, the guard's own meta-tests; the plugin fails any test elsewhere that carries the marker. The meta-tests prove that a 150 ms `time.sleep` inside a coroutine fails, a 50 ms one passes, `await asyncio.sleep(0.5)` passes, and a "Task was destroyed but it is pending" record fails.

### 2.9 The streaming harness

Server side, `docchat_testing.streaming.collect_stream(response)` reads an `httpx` streaming response as bytes, splits on byte `0x0A` before decoding, validates every line against `ChatStreamEvent`, and asserts the order `meta`, `status`*, `delta`*, `citations`, `followups`*, then exactly one terminal event (`done` or `error`), with `heartbeat` allowed anywhere. The recorded streams of `tests/fixtures/streams/` are shared with the web reader tests, which feed them split at every byte offset into two chunks and as 1-byte chunks; a mirror Python test proves the encoder never emits `0x0A` inside an event. Terminal proof: the stub's `cut=` directive makes the API emit an `error` event, and a byte stream with no terminal event makes the reader report an incomplete stream. Disconnect proof: on the live server the client reads `meta` and two `delta` events of a `delay_ms=200` stream, then closes the connection; `GET /_stub/stats` shows `clientDisconnected` incremented within 2 s, and the turn is stored `cancelled` with its partial answer.

### 2.10 Telemetry capture and the canary audit

During every integration session the plugin installs in-memory OpenTelemetry exporters (span, log, metric) through `configure_telemetry(exporters=…)`, the test-only parameter of the telemetry composition function (`85-observability.md` section 1.2). `builders.canary(kind)` embeds `cnry-<kind>-<8 hex>` (kinds `question`, `answer`, `chunk`, `filename`, `fact`, `email`, `displayname`, `instruction`, `comment`, `title`, `summary`). At session end the plugin serialises every exported span (name, attributes, event attributes, `exception.message` and `exception.stacktrace` included), log record (body, attributes) and metric data-point attribute set, and fails the session when any string contains `cnry-`, an e-mail-shaped value or a JWT-shaped value, naming the telemetry item. The per-component canary tests of section 5 are instances of this audit.

### 2.11 The data-access audits

**The chat path** is `createChatTurn` (including the after-response work it starts), `getCitation`, `getCitationContent`, `getCitationDocument`, `getSession` and `putTurnFeedback`.

**Cosmos DB audit.** Integration tests of these operations run with `data_audit.CosmosAudit`, a recording wrapper the composition root installs on the container clients, which records each `read_item`, `query_items`, `create_item`, `upsert_item`, `replace_item`, `patch_item`, `delete_item` and `execute_item_batch` with its partition key, its container and the audit context (the operation, or the after-response step, that made it). A chat-path call without a partition key, or with cross-partition querying enabled, fails the test. A query on only the `[userId]` prefix of `conversations` fails the test unless it is one of the two prefix queries the application makes (`13-conversations-and-memory.md` section 1.12): the session list of `listSessions`, and the extraction re-check query of the after-response task (`13-conversations-and-memory.md` section 6.4 step 2.3, whether that task was scheduled after a completed turn or by the turn-start re-check `ensure_post_turn_work` of section 6.5), which runs after the response, off the request path. A prefix query in any other context, on any other container, or between `begin_turn` and the terminal event fails the test. `data_audit.CosmosFaults`, installed the same way on one named container, fails every call as a connection failure (`azure.core.exceptions.ServiceRequestError`).

**SQL plan audit.** Every repository function used on a request path carries `@request_path(example_params=…)`. `tests/integration/data_access/test_sql_plans.py` seeds 10,000 users, 2,000 projects, 100,000 memberships, 200 logical and 220 physical indexes, 50,000 documents, 100,000 versions, 100,000 chunk sets with served rows, 2,000 runs and 20,000 run items, then runs each registered statement under `SET SHOWPLAN_XML ON` and fails when a plan holds a scan (`Table Scan`, `Clustered Index Scan` or `Index Scan`) that can read more than 1,000 rows. A scan reads at most the rows of the index it reads, which for a filtered index are the rows its filter admits; a scan that a constant `TOP (n)` (neither `PERCENT` nor `WITH TIES`) reads directly, with no residual predicate and only `Compute Scalar` operators between them, reads at most n rows. So every statement on a table above 1,000 rows uses index seeks or bounded range scans: a filtered index's own small set (the provisioning insert's read of `IX_projects_default`, the queue counts on `IX_run_items_claim` and `IX_run_items_running`) and the first page of a list read in index order under its `TOP` (the unfiltered administrator lists) pass, and a scan of a whole large table or of a filtered index that admits more than 1,000 rows fails. The audit writes each example parameter into the statement as a constant of the type the driver binds it as, because SQL Server returns no showplan for a parameterised (RPC) statement; a plan audited this way can match a filtered index the parameterised statement couldn't, so a statement whose plan depends on such a match is written so that the parameterised form keeps it. The full `app.ops_config` read is exempt by name.

### 2.12 The Durable orchestration harness

`docchat_testing.durable.OrchestrationHarness` drives the generator orchestrators of the Durable Functions Python SDK 1.7. It supplies a context implementing the members the orchestrators use (`call_activity`, `call_activity_with_retry`, `call_sub_orchestrator`, `create_timer`, `wait_for_external_event`, `task_any`, `task_all`, `continue_as_new`, `current_utc_datetime`, `is_replaying`, `instance_id`, `get_input`, `set_custom_status`; not `new_uuid`, because orchestrators generate no IDs: the activity that writes a row generates its ID and returns it); records scheduled actions as history; runs activities in process (their real functions, against emulators and fakes) or returns scripted results and failures; advances a virtual clock for timers; and replays the generator from the start after each completed task, as the runtime does. It can fail an activity at step n, simulate a crash (discard the generator and replay from history, proving no completed activity runs a second time), raise external events, and count history events per generation. Its `client()` returns a Durable client double over the harness's instances for the timer functions' bodies (`ingestion_keepalive`, `ingestion_maintenance`, `ensure_dispatcher`): `get_status` reports `runtime_status` (`Running` while the generator waits on a task, `Terminated` once a `terminate` has taken effect), `custom_status` and `last_updated_time`; `start_new`, `raise_event` and `terminate` act on the harness's instances; a `terminate` takes effect at once unless the test sets a virtual delay for it. The virtual clock is the one the timer functions receive as their clock port, so a test runs a tick at a chosen virtual time and the tick's own waits pass in virtual time. The deployed crash-resume test (section 6.7) validates the harness against the real host.

### 2.13 Browser test framework

`tests/e2e` is the private npm package `@docchat/e2e` with `@playwright/test` 1.64.1, `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 and `@azure/static-web-apps-cli` 2.0.10, all exact, and the CLI's `overrides` of `01-repository.md` section 8.1. `playwright.config.ts`: projects `chromium`, `firefox`, `webkit` and `msedge` (`channel: "msedge"`) for `specs/web/`, `chromium` only for `specs/web-deployed/`; `retries` 0 locally and 1 in CI; `trace: "retain-on-failure"`; `forbidOnly: true` in CI; `webServer` starts `swa start <dist> --port 4280` with the `staticwebapp.config.json` from the web client's generator. Every spec installs a `securitypolicyviolation` collector with `addInitScript` and fails on any violation; accessibility scans (`AxeBuilder` after each screen state a test reaches, in both themes) use tags `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa`, `wcag22aa`, and any violation of any impact fails the test. The stub API and stub identity provider, the shared gates fixture and every spec's content are `10-web-client.md` section 13's. Besides the answers that section lists, the stub API serves, when a spec scripts them: a bare `403` with no body on `getCurrentUser`; `503 malware_scanning_not_ready` with `retryAfterSeconds` from `adminPreflightUploads` and `adminUploadContent`; after a `putTurnFeedback` of `{"rating": "up", "category": null, "comment": "<text>"}`, the same stored feedback in `getSession`'s turn; `ProjectMemberChange`, `ProjectIndexMappingChange` and `ProjectPartRemoval` (`200` with a body) carrying `projectRowVersion`; `412 precondition_failed` and `428 precondition_required` on `adminAddProjectMember`, `adminRemoveProjectMember`, `adminMapProjectIndex` and `adminUnmapProjectIndex`; `429 too_many_requests`, `429 write_rate_limited`, `429 rate_limited` and `429 too_many_downloads`, each with `retryAfterSeconds`; and a `getCitationContent` or `getCitationDocument` `200` whose body ends before its `Content-Length`. A real-account sign-in is checked by hand at each release (the release sign-in check of section 5.8), because Conditional Access policies differ per tenant.

## 3. The authorisation matrix test

### 3.1 What it is

`apps/api/tests/integration/test_authorization_matrix.py` (T-001) proves every operation's `401`, `403` and `404` behaviour from the API's own access metadata. It builds the OpenAPI document from the app under test (`app.openapi()` through the API's exporter, never the committed `apps/api/openapi.json`, so an unexported route can't hide) and generates one parametrised pytest case per operation and check. The app under test receives the permissive limiters (section 1.1 item 7), so the hundreds of requests each principal sends never meet the burst or write limit. It runs against SQL Server, Azurite and the Cosmos DB emulator with a seeded world, on every CI run.

### 3.2 Inputs per operation

- `x-docchat-access`: `public`, `user` or `admin` (`11-api.md` section 1.13).
- `x-docchat-scoped-params`: path and query parameter names, and body fields written `body:<JSON pointer>`.
- `x-docchat-error-codes`: the problem codes the operation can return.
- **The request.** The operation's OpenAPI request example when it has one (`11-api.md` section 1.13 lists the twelve operations that carry one), otherwise a body generated from the request schema by `schema_fill`. Every ID field is resolved against the seeded world through a constant name-to-kind map: `projectId` → project, `indexId` → index, `documentId` → document, `versionId` → document version, `runId` → ingestion run, `sessionId` → session, `turnId` → turn, `citationNumber` → citation, `factId` → memory fact, `userId` → user; for the array pointers `initialMemberIds` and `versionIds` the map applies to each element. A scoped name outside the map fails the suite.
- **The seeded world.** Projects A and B, each with a mapped default index (`X` mapped to A, `Y` mapped to B); a document with a superseded and a current version in each index; a completed ingestion run per index; for `alice` (member of A) and `bob` (member of B) one session with two completed turns carrying citations, and one memory fact each; `carol` is `Admin` with no membership; `erin` has no role (section 2.5).

### 3.3 Generation

1. `doc = app.openapi()` through the exporter, from the app under test.
2. For each operation, read the three extensions; a missing one emits the failing case `missing_access_metadata`.
3. Build the request (section 3.2); an identifier that can't be resolved fails the case `unresolved_example`.
4. `public`: emit "anonymous → `200`, empty body" and assert the operation is `GET /healthz` (`getHealth`).
5. Every other operation: no `Authorization` header → `401`; `Bearer not-a-token` → `401`; no header with a 33 KiB body → `401` (never `413`); `erin` → `403 role_not_assigned`.
6. `admin`: `alice` with real IDs → `403 admin_required`; `alice` with random IDs → `403 admin_required`; `alice` with an invalid body → `403 admin_required` (never `404`, `412`, `422` or `428`). For each scoped parameter, `carol` with a random UUID and, where the parameter sits under an `indexId` or `projectId` path parameter, `carol` with an ID of the same kind that belongs to another index or project → the operation's not-found answer (section 3.4), the two bodies byte-identical once `correlationId` is removed.
7. `user`: for each scoped parameter, `alice` with the parameter set to the same kind of resource in project B (or owned by `bob`) → the not-found answer, and with a random UUID → the not-found answer; the two bodies are byte-identical once `correlationId` is removed.
8. Positive control: the unmodified request with `alice` (`user`) or `carol` (`admin`) → `2xx`, proving the refusals are real.
9. Any operation that produced fewer than three cases fails the suite.

### 3.4 The not-found answer

For a path or query parameter the not-found answer is the operation's `404` code for that parameter, from its `x-docchat-error-codes` and its section of `11-api.md`. Three body pointers answer otherwise, exactly as `11-api.md` section 1.13 states; the harness asserts these answers for the unreachable and the random identifier alike:

| Operation | Pointer | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `adminCreateProject` | `body:/initialMemberIds` | `422 validation_failed` with reason `not_found` at `body.initialMemberIds.<i>`; nothing written |
| `adminReserveUpload` | `body:/projectId` | `422 project_not_mapped` with reason `not_allowed` at `body.projectId`; nothing written |
| `adminSubmitUploads` | `body:/versionIds` | `200` with `runId: null` and outcome `not_found` for each such version; no run created |

At an array pointer the harness substitutes the identifier into every element.

### 3.5 Checks the suite enforces

1. Every operation in the document carries all three extensions.
2. The `public` set is exactly `GET /healthz`, which answers an anonymous in-process call `200` with an empty body.
3. Every other operation answers `401` with no `Authorization` header, with `Bearer not-a-token`, and with no header plus a 33 KiB body.
4. Every non-public operation answers `erin` `403 role_not_assigned`.
5. Every `admin` operation answers `alice` `403 admin_required` with real IDs, random IDs and an invalid body.
6. For every scoped parameter, the not-found answer of section 3.4 for both identifiers, with byte-identical bodies apart from `correlationId`.
7. The unmodified request with the right caller answers `2xx`.

The generator's own meta-test (T-565) proves that it fails an app whose route lacks an extension, whose two not-found bodies differ, or whose operation yields fewer than three cases.

## 4. The cross-project and cross-user isolation suite

### 4.1 The registry

`tests/integration/isolation/` (T-002) runs every entry of `docchat_testing.isolation.DATA_PATHS` through the in-process API against the emulators, with `CosmosAudit` installed and the permissive limiters. An entry has:

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `name` | a unique dotted name `<owner>.<data path>.<case>` (the chat pipeline's entries use `<data path>.<case>`) |
| `owner` | the component that registers it: `ia` (identity and access), `chat`, `conv` (conversations and memory), `ing` (ingestion) |
| `data_path` | one of `chat`, `retrieval`, `sessions`, `memory`, `feedback`, `citations`, `documents`, `uploads`, `ingestion_status`, `projects`, `members`, `mappings`, `overrides`, `admin_routes` |
| `actor` | one of the five shared principals of section 2.5, with its fixture role |
| `setup` | builder calls that create the memberships, mappings, sessions, turns and facts the entry uses |
| `action` | the API calls the actor makes |
| `expected` | the answers and, for Cosmos DB, the audited partition keys |
| `negative` | `true` when the expected result is a refusal or an absence, `false` for a positive control |

The registry completeness check (T-003) fails when two entries share a name, when an entry's actor isn't one of the five principals, when an entry names no data path, or when any of the data paths `chat`, `retrieval`, `sessions`, `memory`, `feedback`, `citations`, `documents` and `ingestion_status` has no negative entry. A data path added without a negative entry therefore fails the suite.

### 4.2 Required entries

Each entry's setup makes the memberships it states; otherwise `alice` is a member of A and `bob` of B.

**Identity and access (`ia`).**

| Entry | Expected |
|---|---|
| `ia.projects.member_lists_own` | `alice` lists A and never B in `listMyProjects` |
| `ia.projects.admin_lists_all` (positive) | `carol` lists A and B without membership rows |
| `ia.projects.archived_hidden` | an archived project disappears from both lists and answers `404 project_not_found` on every chat path, for `carol` and `alice` alike |
| `ia.members.revoked_and_restored` | removing `alice` from A makes her next `createChatTurn` in A `404 project_not_found` and hides A's sessions; re-adding her shows them again |
| `ia.members.user_token_refused` | a `User` token on any member operation gets `403 admin_required` with real and random IDs |
| `ia.mappings.mapped_only` | chat in A resolves only an index mapped to A; an index mapped only to B is never used for A |
| `ia.mappings.unmapped_hides_citations` | unmapping an index hides A's citations of that index (`404 citation_not_found`) |
| `ia.overrides.unmapped_refused` | an override for `alice` in A to an index not mapped to A is refused by the composite foreign key `FK_user_index_overrides_mapping` |
| `ia.overrides.expired_ignored` | an expired override is ignored and the project default is used |
| `ia.overrides.other_user_unchanged` | `alice`'s override never changes `bob`'s resolution |
| `ia.admin_routes.user_token_refused` | every `/v1/admin` operation with a `User` token gets `403 admin_required`, never `404`, `412`, `422` or `428` |

**Chat, retrieval and citations (`chat`).** The ten entries `12-chat-pipeline.md` registers (its Acceptance criteria item 66), with `alice` a member of A and C, `bob` a member of A where an entry reads his session or turn there and of B for `retrieval.shared_index`, project B's index mapped only to B, and index X mapped to A and B:

| Entry | Expected |
|---|---|
| `chat.other_project` | `alice` sends `createChatTurn` for project B → `404 project_not_found`; the stub model receives no call |
| `chat.session_other_project` | `alice` sends `createChatTurn` for project A with her own session ID from project C → `404 session_not_found` |
| `chat.foreign_session` | `alice` sends `createChatTurn` for A with `bob`'s session ID in A → `404 session_not_found`, its body identical to the previous entry's apart from `correlationId` |
| `chat.retrieval_scope` (positive) | `alice` asks in A; the stored `turn_trace.retrieval.searchIndexId` is an index mapped to A, and every `kept[].documentId` belongs to that index |
| `retrieval.shared_index` (positive control) | with index X the default of A and of B, `alice` in A and `bob` in B both receive sources from X |
| `retrieval.override_unmapped` | an override for `alice` in A naming project B's logical index fails on the composite foreign key to `app.project_indexes`, and `alice`'s next turn in A uses A's default |
| `citations.foreign_turn` | `alice` calls `getCitation`, `getCitationContent` and `getCitationDocument` with `bob`'s session and turn IDs in A → `404 citation_not_found` on all three |
| `citations.revoked` | after `alice`'s membership in A is removed, the three operations on her own turn in A → `404 citation_not_found`; after it is restored, `200` |
| `citations.superseded` | after a new version of the cited document is ingested, the three operations → `410 document_changed` |
| `citations.unmapped_index` | after the cited index is unmapped from A, the three operations → `404 citation_not_found` |

**Conversations, memory and feedback (`conv`).** `alice` and `bob` are members of A and B; `carol` is `Admin` with no membership; every entry is negative, and `CosmosAudit` checks that every audited partition key is the caller's own.

| Entry | Setup | Action | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| `conv.sessions.list_own_only` | `bob` has 2 sessions in A; `alice` has 1 | `alice` calls `listSessions`, then `listSessions?projectId=<A>` | only `alice`'s session, both times |
| `conv.sessions.other_user` | `bob`'s session S in A | `alice` and `carol` call `getSession(S)` and `listSessionTurns(S)` | `404 session_not_found` for both callers |
| `conv.sessions.revoked_and_restored` | `alice`'s session S in A with 2 turns | remove `alice` from A; call `listSessions`, `getSession(S)`, `listSessionTurns(S)`; add her back; repeat | while revoked: S absent and `404 session_not_found`; after restore: S listed and readable with both turns unchanged |
| `conv.sessions.project_mismatch` | `alice`'s session S in A | `alice` calls `createChatTurn` with `projectId` B and `sessionId` S | `404 session_not_found`; no turn created |
| `conv.memory.other_user_fact` | `bob` has fact F | `alice` and `carol` call `updateMemoryFact(F)` and `deleteMemoryFact(F)` with their own `etag` | `404 memory_fact_not_found`; `bob`'s document unchanged |
| `conv.memory.own_document_only` | `alice` and `bob` each have 2 facts | `alice` and `carol` call `getMemory` and `clearMemory` | each reads and clears only their own document; `bob`'s facts unchanged |
| `conv.feedback.other_user` | `bob`'s completed turn T in session S | `alice` and `carol` call `putTurnFeedback(S, T)` | `404 turn_not_found`; T's `feedback` unchanged |
| `conv.feedback.revoked_and_restored` | `alice`'s completed turn T in A | remove `alice` from A; call `putTurnFeedback`; add her back; repeat | `404 turn_not_found` while revoked; `200` after restore |

**Documents, uploads and ingestion status (`ing`).** All negative:

| Entry | Actor | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| `ing.documents.non_admin_refused` | `alice` | `403 admin_required` on every document, upload and run route of `14-ingestion.md` |
| `ing.documents.other_index_absent` | `carol` | the document list and document reads for index X never return a document or version of index Y (`404 document_not_found`, `404 version_not_found`) |
| `ing.uploads.other_index_refused` | `carol` | upload calls for index X never touch a document or version of index Y: `adminSubmitUploads` under X with Y's version IDs answers outcome `not_found` for each, and a reservation under X never reuses Y's document |
| `ing.ingestion_status.other_index_absent` | `carol` | a run of index Y never appears in a run list filtered to X |

### 4.3 Boundaries of the suite

The suite runs in process. The deployed membership-removal test (T-028) proves revocation through platform authentication with real tokens, and the authorisation matrix (T-001) proves the per-operation status codes; the two in-process suites share the seeded-world builders and never each other's cases.

## 5. Test catalogue

### 5.1 Conventions

1. **One identifier per test.** Every test, check and verdict that another file names as a proof has one identifier `T-nnn` here, with its descriptive name beside it; the other files keep using the name, and this catalogue maps the name to the identifier. A requirement proven at two levels has two identifiers (section 1.1 principle 8).
2. **Identifiers are stable.** An identifier, once published, is never renumbered or reused. Each group of sections 5.2 to 5.14 starts at a number ending in 1, so a group has free numbers up to the next group's first; a new test takes its group's next free number, and when the group has none, the next number above the highest identifier in the catalogue. A withdrawn test keeps its row with the level `withdrawn`.
3. **Names in code.** A Python test function that implements a catalogue entry starts with `test_t<nnn>_` (`test_t001_authorisation_matrix`); a Vitest or Playwright test title starts with `T-<nnn>` (`test("T-349 tampered state lands on /chat", …)`); a CI step that implements a check names the identifier in its `name:`. Several functions or cases implement one identifier when its row lists several cases. The catalogue check (T-569) holds code and catalogue together.
4. **Levels.** `unit`, `component`, `integration`, `contract`, `meta` and `browser` run on every pull request as section 1.2 places them; `CI check` is a step of a `ci.yaml` job (section 10.1); `infrastructure` is a test of `tests/infra/` run by the `infra-tests` job on the compiled templates (section 8.1); `deployed` names the suite of section 6.5 that runs it (`smoke`, `full`, `infra`, `prod_smoke`, `scan_prepare`, `scan`) or the job that runs it; `deploy check` is a step of the deploy workflows of `83-infrastructure.md` section 10; `operator check (dev)` is a `docchat-ops` command a person runs against `dev` under just-in-time membership, at the stage acceptance and on the trigger the row names, its exit code and audit row recorded in that stage's acceptance record; `live` is section 1.6's; `nightly` is a `nightly.yaml` job (section 10.1); `load` is a verdict of section 7; `stage acceptance` and `stage 0` tests run once in the build stage that `90-build-order.md` names and once more whenever their subject changes, with their result recorded in that stage's acceptance record; `rehearsal` is performed in `dev` by an operator following the runbook of the procedure it rehearses (`86-operations.md`) and recorded in `docs/runbooks/rehearsals.md`; `manual (release)` is performed before a production release and recorded in the release checklist (`86-operations.md`); `manual (access review)` is the monthly access review of `86-operations.md`, recorded in its ticket. `manual (cost review)` is read by the product owner from Cost Management after an environment's first full idle month and recorded in the acceptance record of the stage that provisioned that environment.
5. **Traceability.** A row's name is the proof name the owning file's acceptance criterion uses, and the names after `also:` are the other names other files give the same test; the statements of `82-security.md` are cited by their `SEC-nn` numbers and the proof list of `85-observability.md` section 3.7 by its item numbers.

### 5.2 Identity and access (T-001 to T-033)

| ID | Test | Level | Proves |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-001 | the authorisation matrix test | integration | Section 3: for all 45 operations, the three access extensions; `GET /healthz` the only public route (`200`, empty body); `401` without a token, with `Bearer not-a-token` and with a 33 KiB body; `403 role_not_assigned` without a role; `403 admin_required` for a `User` on every `admin` operation with real IDs, random IDs and an invalid body; the not-found answer of section 3.4 for every scoped parameter with byte-identical bodies; `2xx` for the right caller. |
| T-002 | the isolation suite | integration | Section 4: every `DATA_PATHS` entry of identity and access, chat, conversations and ingestion passes, with `CosmosAudit` confirming that every audited partition key is the caller's own. |
| T-003 | the isolation suite's registry completeness check | integration | Every entry has a unique dotted name, a data path and one of the five shared principals as actor, and each of the data paths chat, retrieval, sessions, memory, feedback, citations, documents and ingestion status has at least one negative entry; a path without one fails the suite (section 4.1). |
| T-004 | the token-validator unit tests | unit | `11-api.md` Acceptance criteria item 13: `alg` `none`, `HS256` and `PS256` fail `bad_alg` before any key lookup; `exp` and `nbf` 59 s off pass and 61 s off fail; another issuer, an `aud` array, another audience, `ver` `1.0` and another `tid` fail with their own reasons; a token signed with another key under a known `kid` fails `bad_signature`; `scp` without `access_as_user` fails; missing claims give `missing_claim`; `roles` as a string gives `401 bad_roles`, none of the two roles `403 role_not_assigned`; a 16,385-character token, two `Authorization` headers and the `Basic` scheme fail, lower-case `bearer` passes; every claim override minted by `mint_token` produces its own reason. |
| T-005 | the test-client acceptance unit test | unit | The test client's `azp` is accepted only in `dev`, `load` and `local` with `DOCCHAT_AUTH_TEST_CLIENT_ID` set, and refused in `prod` and when the setting is empty. |
| T-006 | the `x-ms-client-principal` and `x-ms-token` string test (also: the `x-ms-client-principal` string unit test) | unit | No source file of `apps/api` or `packages/core` outside tests contains `x-ms-client-principal` or `x-ms-token` (case-insensitive), and a forged `X-MS-CLIENT-PRINCIPAL-ID` header doesn't change the caller. |
| T-007 | the principal mapping unit tests | unit | A valid token with `roles: ["User"]` yields a `Principal` with the token's `oid`, `tid`, `azp`, `client = web`, `roles = {User}` and `is_admin = false`; `Principal.email` takes `email`, else a `preferred_username` with exactly one `@`, and is empty for values with `#`, whitespace or two `@`. |
| T-008 | the signing-key provider unit tests | unit | An unknown `kid` causes one key-set fetch, a second within 300 s none, 50 concurrent requests one (single flight); the background refresh runs every 3,600 s on the fake clock and is cancelled at shutdown; a set older than 24 hours with a failed fetch gives `503 authentication_unavailable` with `retryAfterSeconds` 30; oversized and keyless responses are refused and keep the last valid set. |
| T-009 | the authorisation semantics unit tests | unit | `authorize_project_for_chat` over {administrator, member, non-member} × {active, archived, missing} allows only (administrator, active) and (member, active), every refusal raising the same `ProjectNotFound`; `resolve_chat_index` uses an unexpired override, ignores an expired one, falls back to the default and raises `project_index_unavailable` with no active physical index; `hasDefaultIndex` and `resolve_chat_index` agree in every index state. |
| T-010 | the authorisation semantics integration tests | integration | After `adminRemoveProjectMember` that person's next chat request in the project answers `404 project_not_found`; an archived project is refused on every chat path for administrators and members alike and a restore brings it back; `listMyProjects` returns `hasDefaultIndex` `false` for a project with no mapping and for one whose default index has no active physical index. |
| T-011 | the access cache unit tests | unit | The access cache holds an entry at most 60 s, evicts the least recently used entry at capacity, and caches only active records. |
| T-012 | the user provisioning integration tests | integration | 32 concurrent first requests for one `oid` create one `users` row, one membership per default project and one `user.provisioned` audit row; first requests of 50 different users at once all succeed, deadlocks resolved by the unit of work's retry. |
| T-013 | the last-seen unit test | unit | `getCurrentUser` makes no SQL call when the caller's last-seen write is under 15 minutes old, apart from the lazy ops-config refresh (at most one per process every 60 s), and writes the last-seen time once it is older. |
| T-014 | the `getCurrentUser` features unit test | unit | `getCurrentUser.features` reflects `memory.enabled` and `feedback.comments_enabled` from the ops-config snapshot. |
| T-015 | the user search integration test | integration | User search matches display-name and e-mail prefixes, treats `%`, `_` and `[` in `q` literally, matches an exact UUID and sets `isMember` for the project given. |
| T-016 | the burst-limiter unit tests | unit | 100 requests in 1 s (test clock) from one `oid` get at least 50 `429 too_many_requests`, each with `retryAfterSeconds` 1 and `Retry-After: 1`, and the refused ones touch no store; the 41st request of one instant is refused; another `oid` is unaffected; `createChatTurn` is never burst-refused; after 1 s idle a refused `oid` gets 10 more; a 50,000-user map evicts the least recently used. |
| T-017 | the write-limiter unit tests | unit | Inside one minute the write window allows request 120 and refuses request 121 with `429 write_rate_limited`; a failing Cosmos DB gives `503 dependency_unavailable`; five ETag conflicts in a row give `429 write_rate_limited` with `retryAfterSeconds` 1. |
| T-018 | the write-limiter emulator integration test | integration | With the real limiters, 130 `putTurnFeedback` calls from one user starting at second 0 of a UTC minute and 0.4 s apart give exactly 120 successes and 10 `429 write_rate_limited` with `retryAfterSeconds` from 1 to 60 and the same `Retry-After`; `wrate:{m}` holds `count` 120 and `ttl` 180. |
| T-019 | the limiter unit tests with an unreachable store | unit | Every rate, concurrency and quota limiter whose store doesn't answer refuses with `503 dependency_unavailable` and `retryAfterSeconds` 5, never allowing the request. |
| T-020 | the audit helper integration test | integration | A forced SQL failure during `write_audit_event_after_commit` retries 3 times, then emits one `audit_write_failed` event and one `docchat.audit.write_failures` increment, while the request still answers `200` with its change committed. |
| T-021 | the audit integration tests | integration | Every audited action writes its `app.audit_events` row in the transaction of its change, with metadata only; a forced audit-insert failure rolls the change back; the upload operations' rows carry the acting administrator. |
| T-022 | the deployed platform-authentication test | deployed smoke | In `dev` (and `load`), platform authentication answers `401` without a token and with another audience's token, `403` for an `azp` that isn't allowed and for a foreign tenant, and `401` for an anonymous `/healthz`, while the health check reports every instance healthy; an anonymous request to any `/v1` route gets `401`. |
| T-023 | the `prod` authentication settings check | deployed (prod deploy check) | The `deploy` job of `prod`, as the `prod` deploy identity, reads `authsettingsV2` through Azure Resource Manager and fails unless it allows exactly the web client. |
| T-024 | the deployed CORS preflight test | deployed smoke | `tests/deployed/test_cors_preflight.py`: the five preflight checks of `11-api.md` section 1.9: a preflight from the web client's origin without a token answers `200` or `204` with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` equal to that origin for `GET` and `PATCH`; a real call without a token from it gets `401`; a preflight from another origin gets no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; a real call with a token gets `200`. |
| T-025 | the deployed correlation-ID test | deployed smoke | A client `traceparent` comes back as the problem body's `correlationId` through platform authentication. |
| T-026 | the deployed access checks | deployed smoke | `outsider` gets `404 project_not_found` on the test project, `norole` `403 role_not_assigned` (skipped with its reason while `DOCCHAT_TEST_NOROLE_SKIP_REASON` is set, always skipped in `load`), `member` `403 admin_required` on an admin route, and `getCurrentUser` for `admin` shows `isAdmin: true`; an ROPC token of a `User` test user gets `200` from `getCurrentUser`. |
| T-027 | the deployed no-role test | deployed smoke | The `dev` no-role test user's token, which carries no `roles` claim, gets `403 role_not_assigned` (part of the deployed access checks, reported separately so its skip reason is visible). |
| T-028 | the deployed membership-removal test | deployed full | After a test user's membership is removed through the admin API, their next `createChatTurn` in that project gets `404 project_not_found` within the 60-second access-cache lifetime; re-adding restores access. |
| T-029 | the Graph registration property checks | deployed smoke | The three `dev` registrations match `83-infrastructure.md` sections 7.2 to 7.4 property by property (token version 2, identifier URI, scope and role GUIDs, pre-authorised applications, assignment required, public client flows off on the web client, no password or certificate credential); `prod` has no test client (checked by the `prod` deploy job). |
| T-030 | the post-provision idempotency test | deployed (stage acceptance) | Running `scripts/identity_post_provision.py` a second time against `dev` changes no grant and no assignment. |

| T-032 | the deployed Cosmos DB group-principal test | deployed (stage acceptance) | A `dev` test operator joins `sg-docchat-dev-conversation-readers`, takes a fresh token and retries a point read of a seeded item every 60 s for up to 15 minutes: `200` keeps group mode, `403` sets per-principal mode in `infra/env/<env>.json`; the outcome is recorded in ADR 0024. |
