### 5.10 Observability (T-481 to T-503)

| ID | Test | Level | Proves |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-481 | the `configure_telemetry` unit tests | unit | `configure_telemetry` sets `OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLER=microsoft.fixed_percentage`, the ratio and `OTEL_PROPAGATORS=tracecontext`, applies the instrumentation options with Live Metrics off, and the resource carries `service.name`, `service.version` and `deployment.environment.name` and no `service.namespace`. |
| T-482 | the no-connection-string unit test | unit | Without a connection string nothing is exported, and the stderr handler still writes scrubbed `WARNING` records. |
| T-483 | the attribute-registry unit tests | unit | `log_event` raises on an unregistered key or a value of the wrong type in test mode and in production drops it and increments `docchat.telemetry.dropped_attributes`; `emit_event` does the same for undeclared events and properties, keeping the rest of the event; every event of `85-observability.md` section 4.12 is declared with exactly its listed properties. |
| T-484 | the dimension unit test | unit | Every instrument's declared dimensions belong to the closed set of `85-observability.md` section 3.5 and none is an identifier. |
| T-485 | the telemetry catalogue unit test | unit | The attribute registry equals a machine-readable copy of `85-observability.md` section 4's tables: every span, metric, event and log record of `11-api.md` section 1.16, `12-chat-pipeline.md` section 19, `13-conversations-and-memory.md` section 11.4 and `14-ingestion.md` section 18.4 with its name, attributes, unit and dimensions, and no other name. |
| T-486 | the scrubbing unit tests | unit | `scrub` replaces one sample of each of the 8 patterns (JWT, Bearer and Basic credentials, connection-string secrets, signed query parameters, a 512-bit base64 key, named secret assignments, a PEM private key, an email address) and leaves UUIDs, trace IDs, SHA-256 values, ISO timestamps and numbers unchanged; each attach point scrubs its fields; URL queries are stripped and `db.statement`, `url.query` and every header attribute except `content-type`, `content-length` and `traceparent` removed; an internal error yields `[redacted:error]` and a `docchat.telemetry.scrub_errors` increment. |
| T-487 | the exception-recording unit test | unit | An exception whose message holds a canary exports no canary when recorded by `record_failure`, by `span.record_exception` as instrumentation does, or logged with `exc_info`; the stack trace keeps frame lines and qualified type names of the exception, its cause and an exception-group member, and drops every message line, continuation line and note. |
| T-488 | the exception-text canary test | integration | A log record and a span carrying a fake JWT, a fake connection string and an email address reach the in-memory exporters redacted; an exception whose message is `cnry-question-<8 hex>` raised in an unmapped API route (answered `500`), in a post-turn background task and in an ingestion activity leaves no exported span event, log record or stack trace containing `cnry-`. |
| T-489 | the session-wide canary audit | integration | At the end of every integration session, no exported span, log record, event or metric data point contains `cnry-`, an email-shaped value or a JWT-shaped value (section 2.10). Also named "the telemetry capture test". |
| T-490 | the SQL layer telemetry test | integration | No span, metric or log record from the SQL layer contains statement text or a canary parameter value. |
| T-491 | the sampling unit test | unit | At ratio 0.5 a request is exported exactly when its trace ID falls inside the ratio, whatever its `traceparent` flag; at 1.0 everything is exported; metrics are exported at every ratio. |
| T-492 | the ingestion trace integration test | integration | An upload request's trace contains the starter span and every activity span of its run, the queue message carries `traceparent` and `tracestate`, and the dispatcher's sweep and claim spans are roots linked to the run's trace. |
| T-493 | the correlation-ID integration test | integration | A problem body's `correlationId` and the `meta` and `error` events' `correlationId` equal the trace ID of the request's `traceparent`. |
| T-494 | the deployed trace lookup check | deployed (`full` suite) | `docchat-ops --env dev trace show` with a smoke request's trace ID lists that request's spans. |
| T-495 | the ingestion start-up unit test | unit | Importing `docchat_ingestion.function_app` with passing start-up checks emits exactly one `ingestion_worker_started` per process, with no properties, whose resource carries `service.name` `docchat-ingestion` and `service.version` = `DOCCHAT_BUILD_COMMIT`; a failing check emits none and one `job_failed`; `configure_telemetry` itself emits no event for either service. |
| T-496 | the loop-lag unit test | unit | The loop-lag monitor records about 0 ms on an idle loop, at least 150 ms after a 200 ms blocking call, and stops at shutdown. |
| T-497 | the deployed telemetry check | deployed (stage acceptance, `dev`) | With `disableLocalAuth: true` on the component, records of both apps arrive in `appi-docchat-dev` under `AppRoleName` `docchat-api` and `docchat-ingestion` with `AppVersion` equal to the deployed commit. |
| T-498 | the Bicep build test of `workbooks.bicep` | infrastructure | Each environment gets the nine workbooks of `85-observability.md` section 5.1 with their names, display names and source; every workbook JSON parses, has the `TimeRange` parameter, uses it in every query and uses only the documented placeholders. |
| T-499 | the query check | live (`dev`, stage acceptance) | Every workbook tile query and every alert query runs without a KQL error against `log-docchat-dev` through the Log Analytics query API (`docchat_testing.logquery.query_logs`, timespan `P1D`). |
| T-500 | the alert module unit test | infrastructure | `alerts.bicep` built for `dev`, `prod` and `load` yields 41, 44 and 0 rules, each named `docchat-<env>-<slug>` with its window, frequency, severity, mute, `autoMitigate`, action group `ag-docchat-<env>-ops` and a description starting with its ID and runbook title; metric rules use only allowed windows (PT1M, PT5M, PT15M, PT30M, PT1H, PT6H, PT12H, P1D) and frequencies (PT1M, PT5M, PT15M, PT30M, PT1H); A06, A41 and A42 take their thresholds from `alerts.ttftP95Ms`, `alerts.openAiFirstChunkP95Ms` and `alerts.openAiCallMeanMs`. |
| T-501 | the alert query unit tests | unit | Each rate rule's KQL runs over constant `datatable` inputs at and around its guard and threshold: A27 with 1 abandoned of 19 finalised turns returns no row and with 2 of 20 returns a row. A58's KQL over constant `ingestion_item_failed` rows returns one row for each event with `operation` `rerender` and `outcomeCode` `render_failed` or `render_too_large`, and none for `operation` `ingest` with `render_failed` or for `operation` `rerender` with `storage_unavailable`. |
| T-502 | the bootstrap template check of security alerts | infrastructure | The bootstrap template declares the eleven security alerts A44 to A54 in `rg-docchat-identity` with the names, types, windows, mutes and severities of `85-observability.md` section 6.6, each routed to `ag-docchat-security`, and no environment template declares a security alert or a second security action group. |
| T-503 | the alert runbook-map check | infrastructure | Every alert rule of the compiled `alerts.bicep` for `dev` and `prod` has a row in the table of runbook "Alert response index and runbook maps" in `docs/runbooks/`, naming the runbook that the rule's description names. |

### 5.11 Security gates and repository checks (T-511 to T-534)

| ID | Test | Level | Proves |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-511 | the repository-configuration test | unit | `tests/support/tests/test_repository_config.py` checks the tree of `01-repository.md` section 1, the workspace members and their `requires-python` and `[build-system]`, the byte-exact ignore files and `.env.example`, the ingestion requirements export (CPU `torch` wheel with its SHA-256, no `nvidia-*` line), the pre-commit `rev` SHAs, the gitleaks allowlist, `.github/dependabot.yml`, and every version, tag, digest and hash of `01-repository.md` section 10 against the files that apply it. |
| T-512 | the `lint` job (also: the CI lint and type-check jobs) | CI check | `uv lock --locked` and `uv sync --locked --all-packages --all-groups` succeed (a `pyproject.toml` change without its `uv.lock` change fails), and Ruff (with rule `S608`, which fails string-built SQL), mypy and every pre-commit hook pass. |
| T-513 | the import contracts | CI check | `uv run lint-imports` passes contracts 1 to 7, and the contract fixtures (a `docchat_core` module importing `docchat_api`, a module other than `docchat_core.sql.engine` importing `pyodbc`, a route module importing `azure.identity.aio`) each break a contract. |
| T-514 | the `check_outbound_clients.py` fixture tests | unit | The check fails, naming file and line, on `httpx.AsyncClient(` without `timeout=`, `follow_redirects=True`, `urlopen(` without `timeout=`, an Azure SDK client in `docchat_core.clients` without its timeouts, an Azure client class built outside `docchat_core.clients` and an `azure.identity` import outside contract 6, and passes the repository. |
| T-515 | the test layout check | unit | `check_test_layout.py` fails for a module without its mirrored `test_<name>.py`, naming it, and passes a module exempt with a reason. |
| T-516 | the forbidden-files check | unit | `check_forbidden_files.py` fails, naming the path, when a temporary repository tracks one path of each forbidden pattern of `01-repository.md` section 1.12, a `.env` forced past `.gitignore` included. |
| T-517 | the untyped-imports check | unit | `check_mypy_untyped.py` fails a `pyproject.toml` whose untyped-imports override lists a `docchat_*` module or that adds a second override setting `follow_untyped_imports` or `ignore_missing_imports`, and passes the committed file. |
| T-518 | the forbidden-extra unit test | unit | Every input model reachable from the OpenAPI document and every registered queue-message, Cosmos DB document and settings model has `extra="forbid"`. |
| T-519 | the coverage gate | CI check | The `python-tests` job fails a package below its threshold of section 10.4 and the `web` job a web metric below its threshold, naming the package or metric. |
| T-520 | the `python-tests` job | CI check | `docker compose up -d --wait` with a generated SQL password, then the unit and integration suites with coverage, the same commands `01-repository.md` section 3.6 gives a developer, pass on `main`. |
| T-521 | the SQL unit-of-work unit tests | unit | Error 1205 re-runs the unit of work and succeeds on attempt 2 (`docchat.sql.retries` counting 1); error 40613 is retried at most 12 times within 90 s on the fake clock, then raises `SqlUnavailable`; `retry="none"` never retries; error 2627 raises `SqlUniqueViolation` with the constraint name and no server message text; 50 concurrent units with an expired token make one `get_token` call; the access-token attribute is a 4-byte little-endian length followed by the UTF-16-LE token. |
| T-522 | the data-access integration tests | integration | In `tests/integration/data_access/` under the loop guard: two transactions forced into a deadlock both complete; after 10 minutes idle (fake clock) the pool is disposed and the next unit reconnects; the start-up warm-up opens one session per SQL thread, which the next unit reuses (`01-repository.md` section 6.2 item 11); on a pool warmed as start-up warms it, 200 concurrent `fetch_one` calls on 8 SQL threads never block the loop over 100 ms; cancelling the awaiting coroutine mid-transaction still commits; every session carries `APP=docchat-<service>`. |
| T-523 | the `images` job | CI check | Each image builds with its last step under `--network=none`; the API image's `Config.Cmd` is exactly `["gunicorn","--config","/app/gunicorn.conf.py","docchat_api.app:create_app()"]` with an empty `Config.Entrypoint`; the API and model-stub images run as `10001:10001`; bases are the pinned tags and digests; the ODBC driver exists in the API and ingestion images; both tokenizer encodings load from `/opt/docchat/tiktoken` offline; no image holds a test file, a source map, `.env` or `.azure/`; no first-party image sets `PYTHON_APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_ENABLE_TELEMETRY`. |
| T-524 | the Dockerfile unit test | unit | Every Dockerfile's `FROM` lines are digest-pinned, the API and stub final stages run as UID 10001, the ingestion image defines the 16 parse users `docchat-parse-0` to `docchat-parse-15` with UIDs and GIDs 10002 to 10017, creates `/run/docchat-parse/<uid>.lock` for each with owner root and mode 0600, creates `/run/docchat-scratch` with owner root and mode 0711, removes write access for others from every path but `/tmp` and `/var/tmp`, and installs `libseccomp2` and `procps`, every final stage ends with its smoke `RUN`, and `.dockerignore` excludes the listed paths. |
| T-525 | the Trivy scans | CI check | Trivy scans every layer of every image for vulnerabilities and secrets in the `images` job and nightly, gated by `security_gate.py`. |
| T-526 | the gitleaks scans | CI check | `gitleaks git --redact` over each pull request's commits and nightly over the full history finds no secret outside the allowlist. |
| T-527 | the security gate unit tests | unit | `security_gate.py` fails on a high Trivy finding even when a waiver lists it, passes a waived moderate npm finding, fails on an expired or over-90-day waiver, a reason under 20 characters and an approver outside `approvers.yaml`, rates a `PYSEC-` finding from its `GHSA-` alias, fails on a finding no record rates, and fails on a missing report or an npm `error` object; with the DS002 exception of section 9.3, passes DS002 in `trivy-ingestion.json` and on `apps/ingestion/Dockerfile` in `trivy-config.json`, still fails DS002 in `trivy-api.json`, `trivy-model-stub.json` or on another Dockerfile and any other HIGH or CRITICAL finding of the ingestion image; with an image's `unfixed-os-packages` exception, passes a HIGH or CRITICAL OS-package vulnerability without a fix in that image's report, still fails one with a fixed version or status `fixed`, a language-package finding, a secret, a misconfiguration and another image's finding, and fails in the nightly `build` gate once a finding gains a fix; fails an exception past its review date, refuses a `review_by` beyond 184 days (DS002) or 90 days (`unfixed-os-packages`) after `added`, an entry for another image and a malformed entry or file, and warns 14 days before a review date. |

## 6. Deployed tests and test identities

Deployed tests run against a provisioned environment through its public endpoints, with tokens of cloud-only test users obtained by the resource-owner password (ROPC) grant of `11-api.md` section 1.2 against the environment's test client. `prod` has no test client (`83-infrastructure.md` section 7.4), so `prod` gets anonymous checks only.

### 6.1 Test users and aliases

A User Administrator creates the test users and groups once per tenant and a Privileged Role Administrator creates the administrative unit `DocChat test users` and adds the users to it (runbook "First admins and test identities", part A), in the main tenant, with user principal names `<name>@<domain>`, the domain held in the GitHub environment variable `DOCCHAT_TEST_USER_DOMAIN` of `dev-tests` and `load-tests`.

| Alias | User | Group | API role | Environment |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `admin` | `docchat-test-admin` | `sg-docchat-dev-test-admins` | `Admin` | `dev` |
| `member` | `docchat-test-member` | `sg-docchat-dev-test-users` | `User` | `dev` |
| `outsider` | `docchat-test-outsider` | `sg-docchat-dev-test-users` | `User` | `dev` |
| `norole` | `docchat-test-norole` | `sg-docchat-dev-test-norole` | default access only, so its token carries no `roles` | `dev` |
| `load-01` to `load-20` | `docchat-load-01` to `docchat-load-20` | `sg-docchat-load-test-users` | `User` | `load` |
| `load-admin` | `docchat-load-admin` | `sg-docchat-load-test-admins` | `Admin` | `load` |

Assignment is required on all three registrations of an environment (`83-infrastructure.md` section 7), so every test group is also assigned to the environment's test client: the `dev` groups once, by the post-provision identity step of `dev`; the `load` groups to each load run's new registrations at the run's access gate (section 7.4 job 2). The aliases are the values of `testUserAliases` (`81-config.md` section 4.7) and the names of the secrets that hold the passwords.

When Microsoft Graph refuses the default-access assignment of `sg-docchat-dev-test-norole` on the API (`400`, because the API declares app roles), the post-provision step exits 2 after every other step; a repository administrator then sets the `dev-tests` variable `DOCCHAT_TEST_NOROLE_SKIP_REASON` to `graph_default_access_refused`, and the deployed no-role check (T-027) reports itself skipped with that reason, the token-validator unit tests (T-004) and the authorisation matrix (T-001) remaining the proof of `403 role_not_assigned`.

Two Conditional Access policies of the tenant (runbook "First admins and test identities") apply: every policy that requires multi-factor sign-in excludes the five test groups, and the policy `DocChat test users: non-production APIs only` blocks the five test groups from every resource except the non-production API and test-client registrations, so the exclusion yields tokens for non-production APIs only.

### 6.2 Credentials

- Every test password, of both sets, is the secret `test-user-<alias>` in the shared test vault `kv-docchat-tst-<sharedToken>` (`83-infrastructure.md` section 2), readable only by the CI test identity `id-docchat-citest`, whose federated credentials trust only the GitHub environments `dev-tests` and `load-tests`.
- By default (`ciPrivateNetworking: false`) the vault answers a job's standard GitHub-hosted runner only inside that job's read window, between `firewall.py open --target vault` and `close`; under the private-networking option it answers only on its private endpoint in `snet-ci-private-endpoints`, so only jobs on the runner group `docchat-ci-private` reach it (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 4.6 and 10.16; ADR 0122).
- Each password is 32 characters drawn with `secrets.choice` from letters, digits and `!#%+-.:=?@_~`, written with an expiry of 30 days by `docchat-ops testusers rotate` (`86-operations.md` section 2.8.8; runbook "Test-user password rotation"), every 30 days and at once after a suspected exposure.
- The nightly `test-credential-expiry` job (T-545) warns for every secret expiring within 7 days and fails for an expired one.

### 6.3 Jobs and identities

A job signs in as exactly one identity. Jobs that hold test tokens or read the test vault run in `dev-tests` or `load-tests` as `id-docchat-citest` with `runs-on: ${{ vars.DOCCHAT_CI_PRIVATE_NETWORKING == 'true' && fromJSON('{"group":"docchat-ci-private"}') || 'ubuntu-24.04' }}` and `permissions {contents: read, id-token: write}`, and join no concurrency group for the vault. By default they open and close only the test vault's runner rule, around their vault reads, and end with `firewall.py close --target vault --env <env>` under `if: always()`; under the private-networking option they open, close or sweep no network rule. They touch no other network rule. Work that needs a deploy identity runs in its own job in GitHub environment `dev` or `load`, and passes the token-holding jobs only non-secret job outputs (base URLs, client IDs, the API scope, resource names, run and item IDs, timestamps). Jobs that run `npm ci` hold no `id-token`.

`.github/workflows/deployed-tests.yaml` is `workflow_call` and `workflow_dispatch` with the inputs `environment` (`dev` | `load` | `prod`), `suite` (`smoke` | `full` | `infra` | `prod_smoke` | `scan_prepare` | `scan`; `infra`, `scan_prepare` and `scan` on `dev` only), `stage_acceptance` (boolean, default `false`; `true` only with `environment: dev` and `suite` `full` or `infra`, any other combination failing the run in its first step before any sign-in) and, for `prod` only, the public values `api_base_url`, `web_base_url`, `web_client_id` and `api_scope` (passed by `deploy.yaml` from its `deploy-prod` job's outputs, or copied by an operator from `azd env get-values -e prod`). It is called by `deploy.yaml` after each deploy, by `nightly.yaml` and by `load-test.yaml`.

| Job | Runs for | GitHub environment, runner | Identity | Timeout | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `endpoints` | `dev`, `load` | `dev` on `ubuntu-24.04` (`dev`); `load-tests` on the test runner (`load`; it reads no secret and opens no rule) | the `dev` deploy identity; `id-docchat-citest` in `load` | 10 min | `azure/login`, `azd auth login`, `azd env new <env>`, `azd env set AZURE_RESOURCE_GROUP rg-docchat-<env>`, `azd env refresh -e <env> --no-prompt`; outputs `api_base_url`, `web_base_url`, `web_client_id`, `test_client_id`, `api_scope`, `ingestion_app_name` and `workspace_id` |
| `pytest` | `smoke`, `full`, `scan_prepare`, `scan` | `dev-tests` or `load-tests`, the test runner | `id-docchat-citest` | 30 min (`smoke`, `scan_prepare`), 90 min (`full`), 300 min (`scan`) | `uv run pytest tests/deployed --deployed -m "<suite> and not resume"`, with `--stage-acceptance` appended when the input `stage_acceptance` is `true`; the session fixture runs the token step of section 6.4; last, by default, `firewall.py close --target vault` under `if: always()` |
| `resume-start` | `full` on `dev` | `dev-tests`, the test runner | `id-docchat-citest` | 60 min | section 6.7 step 1; last, by default, `firewall.py close --target vault` under `if: always()` |
| `resume-restart` | `full` on `dev`; needs `resume-start` and `endpoints` | `dev`, `ubuntu-24.04` | the `dev` deploy identity | 90 min | section 6.7 step 2 |
| `infra-checks` | `infra` on `dev`; needs `endpoints` | `dev`, `ubuntu-24.04`, concurrency group `docchat-env-dev` with `cancel-in-progress: false` | the `dev` deploy identity | 60 min | first `uv run --frozen python scripts/deploy/firewall.py sweep --target sql --env dev` (`83-infrastructure.md` section 4.6 item 1), then `uv run pytest tests/deployed --deployed -m infra` (`tests/deployed/test_infrastructure.py`), with `--stage-acceptance` appended when the input `stage_acceptance` is `true`, section 8.2 |
| `prod-smoke` | `prod_smoke` | none | none (`permissions {contents: read}`) | 15 min | `uv run pytest tests/deployed --deployed -m prod_smoke` with the `prod` inputs |
| `web-deployed` | `smoke` on `dev` and `prod_smoke` | none | none, no `id-token` | 20 min | `npm ci` in `tests/e2e`, then Playwright `specs/web-deployed/` on `chromium` with the environment's `web_base_url`, `api_base_url`, `web_client_id`, `api_scope` and the repository variable `AZURE_TENANT_ID` (T-348) |

`infra-checks` joins `docchat-env-dev` because it opens and closes the SQL runner rule with `firewall.py`, which that group serialises. `nightly.yaml` calls the workflow with `environment: dev`, `suite: full` every night and with `suite: infra` every Sunday, never with `stage_acceptance`.

**Identities of the stage-acceptance rows.** `--stage-acceptance` only stops a stage-acceptance function from skipping; each row runs where the identity it needs is held. A row that needs a person's identity is carried out by the stage's performer holding the role `83-infrastructure.md` section 6 names for it, and its result goes into the stage's acceptance record (section 6.5).

| Row | Identity it needs | Where it runs |
|---|---|---|
| T-030 | a tenant administrator (Cloud Application Administrator or Application Administrator), for the Microsoft Graph calls of `scripts/identity_post_provision.py` (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 6.9 and 7.5) | person-run: the stage-0 performer, holding one of those two roles, runs the script against `dev` a second time with the arguments of the first run; its summary names no created or changed grant and no created assignment |
| T-031 | a member of `sg-docchat-dev-config-operators` for the SQL role `docchat_config_operator`, then a person outside it (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 6.9 and 6.13) | person-run: the stage-0 performer, an operator listed in the bootstrap configuration's `operators`, runs `config show` and `config set` from an address in `dev`'s operator allowlist while an approver's just-in-time grant at level `config` is active (runbook "Change an ops-config value"); after the approver revokes it, the performer signs in again for a fresh token and runs `config show`, which exits 3 |
| T-282 | a member of `sg-docchat-operators`, whose Log Analytics Reader on `log-docchat-dev` (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 6.9 and 6.13) permits the Logs queries; the turn itself is the one the deployed chat test (T-281) makes as `member` in the `full` run of `90-build-order.md` section 1.3.1 step 2 | person-run: once that run has ended, the stage's performer, signed in with `az login`, runs `uv run python -m docchat_testing.logquery chat-telemetry --workspace-id <DOCCHAT_LOG_ANALYTICS_WORKSPACE_ID of .azure/dev/.env> --since <the run's start time, UTC>` from their machine; with the performer's `AzureCliCredential` it polls every 60 s for at most 15 minutes for an `AppDependencies` row of `AppRoleName` `docchat-api` and `Name` `docchat.chat.turn` carrying `Properties["docchat.ttft_ms"]`, and `AppMetrics` rows named `docchat.openai.tokens` and `docchat.openai.estimated_cost`, each with `TimeGenerated` after `--since` and `ingestion_time() - TimeGenerated` at most 15 minutes; it exits 0 when all three are found and 1 with `telemetry_missing` naming each item not found; the performer records the exit code and the run ID |
| T-432 | `id-docchat-pr`, whose federated credential trusts only the `pull_request` subject (`83-infrastructure.md` section 6.1) | the step `pr-write-refused` of the `ci.yaml` job `preflight-whatif` on every same-repository pull request (`83-infrastructure.md` section 10.5), so its level is `CI check` |
| T-440 | the `dev` deploy identity, which creates the contained user as the SQL server's Entra administrator, and the ingestion identity, which signs in | the `postprovision` hook of the `dev` deploy job runs `sql_principals.py` from the client ID and, after login error 18456, from the object ID (`83-infrastructure.md` section 6.12); the spike build's `SELECT USER_NAME()` signs in as the ingestion identity; person-run reading: the stage-0 performer, an operator with Log Analytics Reader on `log-docchat-dev` through `sg-docchat-operators`, reads that query's outcome in `appi-docchat-dev` and records the working SID source |
| T-451 | the `dev` deploy identity: the sentinel statement as a member of `sg-docchat-dev-sql-admins`, the Logs queries against `log-docchat-dev` and the read of the `master` database's diagnostic settings through Contributor | `infra-checks`, marker `infra` |
| T-497 | the `dev` deploy identity, for the Logs queries against `log-docchat-dev` through Contributor | `infra-checks`, marker `infra` |

### 6.4 Token acquisition

1. The job runs in `dev-tests` or `load-tests` on the test runner of `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.16, signed in to the Azure CLI as `id-docchat-citest` and as no other identity in any step. By default (`DOCCHAT_CI_PRIVATE_NETWORKING` not `true`) `load_passwords` opens the vault's read window itself: before its first vault read it runs `uv run --frozen python scripts/deploy/firewall.py open --target vault --env <env>` as a child process (`asyncio.create_subprocess_exec`, from the repository root), and after its last read it runs `firewall.py close --target vault --env <env>` in a `finally`, so the runner rule exists only while the passwords are read (`83-infrastructure.md` section 4.6 item 5). Under the private-networking option it opens nothing: the vault's name resolves to its private endpoint inside the runner group's network.
2. `docchat_testing.tokens.load_passwords(vault_uri, aliases)` first awaits the job's GitHub OIDC assertion: `httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(10.0), follow_redirects=False).get(f"{ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=api://AzureADTokenExchange", headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN}"})`, reading `value`. It then builds `azure.identity.aio.ClientAssertionCredential(tenant_id, client_id=<DOCCHAT_CI_TEST_CLIENT_ID>, func=lambda: assertion)` (the assertion is already awaited, so the synchronous `func` returns a string) and reads `test-user-<alias>` for each alias from `DOCCHAT_TEST_VAULT_URI` into memory with the async `SecretClient`, all reads bounded together at 120 s; client and credential are then closed. A later vault read in the same job builds a new credential from a freshly fetched assertion and, by default, opens a new window. An expired secret fails with `test_credentials_expired`; one expiring within 7 days adds a `::warning::` annotation.
3. A vault that can't be reached fails the token step with `test_vault_unreachable` before any test runs. By default: `firewall.py open` exiting non-zero, or a read answered `403 ForbiddenByFirewall` after `open` succeeded and again after `load_passwords` has run `open` once more (another job's concurrent write can undo a rule, `83-infrastructure.md` section 4.6 item 5). Under the private-networking option: no runner of the group in the network, a missing private endpoint or zone link, or a read answered `403 ForbiddenByFirewall` (runbook "Repair the CI runner group"). A job never falls back from one path to the other.
4. ROPC per alias with `httpx` (timeout 15 s; 2 retries on connection errors and `5xx`, none on `4xx`). Failures name their runbook: `AADSTS50126` "password mismatch: run runbook Test-user password rotation"; `AADSTS50076` or `AADSTS50079` "multi-factor sign-in demanded: the Conditional Access exclusion is missing (runbook First admins and test identities)"; `AADSTS53003` "blocked by Conditional Access: the run's API or test client is missing from the test-user policy's exclusions (runbook Run the load test)"; `AADSTS50105` "user not assigned to the application (runbook First admins and test identities)"; `AADSTS65001` or `AADSTS90094` "admin consent missing for the test client (runbook First admins and test identities)".
5. Each token's claims are decoded without signature checking and compared with the alias's expectations (`aud` the API client ID, `scp` containing `access_as_user`, `roles` as assigned); a mismatch fails before any test runs.
6. `::add-mask::<token>` is printed for every token before any other output. Tokens stay in memory, re-acquired with the in-memory password within 5 minutes of `exp`; passwords never leave the job's memory, and `docchat-load tokens` writes tokens only, never passwords, to its mode-0600 output file.
7. Every API call of the suites goes through `docchat_testing.apiclient.DeployedApiClient` (Python) or `lib.js` (k6), which send the bearer token and a fresh `traceparent`; a `429` whose problem `code` is `too_many_requests` or `write_rate_limited` is retried once after `retryAfterSeconds` (at most 120 s), and a second `429`, or a `429` with any other code (`rate_limited`, `too_many_streams`, `token_quota_exceeded`, `too_many_downloads`, `upload_busy`), goes back to the test as its outcome (T-582, T-581).

### 6.5 Suites

| Suite | Runs | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| `smoke` | after every `dev` deploy (`deploy.yaml` job `test-dev`) and after load seeding (section 7.4 job 5); at most 30 minutes | T-022, T-024, T-025, T-026, T-027, T-059, T-281, T-271, T-064, T-029; in `load` also T-597 |
| `full` | nightly on `dev`, and on dispatch | `smoke`, plus T-028, T-586, T-587, T-175, T-177, T-240, T-248, T-448, T-494 |
| `infra` | weekly on `dev` (Sunday, `nightly.yaml`), after a change under `infra/` reaches `dev`, and on dispatch | section 8.2 |
| `prod_smoke` | after every `prod` deploy (`deploy.yaml` job `test-prod`); no token | T-588 and job `web-deployed` |
| `scan_prepare`, `scan` | `dev` only, at the ingestion stage's acceptance and on dispatch | T-158 |

In `load` the suite maps `admin` to `load-admin`, `member` to `load-01` and `outsider` to `load-02` (not a member of `Automated tests`), and always skips the `norole` case, because the `load` set has no user without a role. A suite that fails fails its calling job, and `deploy.yaml` stops before `prod` when `test-dev` fails.

**The malware-scanning suites.** `scan_prepare` runs while scanning is off and creates, idempotently by name, the index `scan-tests` mapped to project `Automated tests`, holding one byte-unique `headings.docx`. `scan` runs once `storage.malwareScanning` is `true` in `infra/env/dev.json` and a `clean` canary exists: it uploads the EICAR test file, built at run time as a ZIP so that no repository file holds it, and a byte-unique `tables.pdf`, polls for at most 245 minutes, re-ingests `scan-tests` after switching its `overlapPercent`, then deletes the two documents it uploaded; the performer returns `storage.malwareScanning` to `false` afterwards, pass or fail.

**The stage-acceptance tests.** The rows of section 5 whose level reads `deployed (stage acceptance)` or `deployed (stage 0)` are functions of `tests/deployed/` that skip unless `--stage-acceptance` is given, with the marker `full` when the `pytest` job runs them and `infra` when `infra-checks` does (section 6.3), run by the performer of the stage named in `90-build-order.md` through `deployed-tests.yaml` dispatched with `stage_acceptance: true` and `suite: full`, then `suite: infra`; those that need a person (the alert tests T-471, the operator-read audit test T-033, the Cosmos DB group-principal test T-032, and T-030, T-031, T-282 and the reading of T-440 as section 6.3 assigns them) are carried out by that person as their rows state, and their results recorded in the stage's acceptance record.

### 6.6 Seed data

The deployed session fixture creates, idempotently and by name, through the admin API as `admin`: the non-default project `Automated tests` with `member` as its member, and the index `automated-tests` (the page-wise method at its default parameters) mapped as its default and holding the four fixture documents of section 1.5, ingested and `completed`. The idempotency suite uses its own index `automated-tests-idempotency` and the crash-resume test `automated-tests-resume`, both mapped to the same project. Every document a test uploads beyond these is deleted by the test that uploaded it.

### 6.7 The crash-resume test

T-587 runs as two jobs, because the restart needs the `dev` deploy identity and the uploads need test tokens:

1. `resume-start`: the token step for `admin`; `uv run pytest tests/deployed/test_ingestion_resume.py --deployed -m resume` ensures, idempotently by name, the index `automated-tests-resume` mapped to `Automated tests`; deletes every document earlier runs left in it and waits until their delete runs end; uploads a generated 120-page PDF `resume-<GitHub run ID>-large.pdf` and submits it alone (run 1); uploads the four fixture documents made byte-unique as `docchat-load build-corpus` does (the line `%docchat-resume-<run ID>-<n>` after `%%EOF`, or the ZIP archive comment) and submits them together (run 2), so at `dev`'s parallelism of 1 run 2 waits behind run 1; polls `adminGetIngestionRun` for run 1 every 15 s until the large item's `stage` is `embed` or later (at most 45 minutes; the test fails when the item ends `failed` or run 1 ends before `embed` was seen); writes `reports/resume.json` (`{runIds, largeItemId, embedSeenAt}`), whose IDs become the job outputs `run_ids` and `large_item_id`.
2. `resume-restart`: `az functionapp restart --name <ingestion_app_name> --resource-group rg-docchat-dev` at once, `restarted_at` being the UTC time the command returned; then `uv run python -m docchat_testing.resume_check --workspace-id <workspace_id> --run-ids <run_ids> --restarted-at <restarted_at>` polls the Log Analytics query API every 60 s, for at most 60 minutes, until both runs have an `ingestion_run_finished` event (`resume_timeout` otherwise), and evaluates:

```kusto
let runs = dynamic([<run IDs>]); let tr = datetime(<restarted_at>);
let a = AppDependencies
  | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d) and AppRoleName == "docchat-ingestion" and Name == "docchat.ingestion.activity"
  | where tostring(Properties["docchat.run_id"]) in (runs)
  | project item = tostring(Properties["docchat.item_id"]), activity = tostring(Properties["docchat.activity_name"]),
            st = TimeGenerated, en = TimeGenerated + DurationMs * 1ms, Success;
a | where Success == true and en < tr
  | join kind=inner (a | where st > tr) on item, activity
  | count
```

R-a: both `ingestion_run_finished` events have `state` `completed` and `itemsSucceeded` = `itemsTotal`; R-b: the query returns 0, so no activity that completed before the restart ran a second time; R-c: `a | where st > tr | count` returns at least 1, so the restart interrupted work still to do. The job fails when any of them fails (T-585 proves the evaluation on recorded rows).

## 7. Load test

The load test proves the scaling targets on a short-lived environment with production SKUs and a stubbed model. Its verdicts are computed by code from k6 thresholds, Log Analytics queries, platform metrics and the Activity Log; no criterion is read by eye.

### 7.1 Tool

- **k6 2.3.0** (confirmed 2026-10-14) from the official release tarball, pinned in `tests/load/k6.lock.json`: `{"version": "2.3.0", "url": "https://github.com/grafana/k6/releases/download/v2.3.0/k6-v2.3.0-linux-amd64.tar.gz", "sha256": "39c3117b6af817592dcd0ce4242105c0a7af10948c2a425306f0be8f7a8a8ab1", "member": "k6-v2.3.0-linux-amd64/k6"}`; `check_k6_lock.py` (T-580) holds the lock to the release's checksums file.
- **Why k6.** Its arrival-rate executors give an open-model load whose rate doesn't fall when the server slows, which the cheap-endpoint and scale-out criteria need; its thresholds are declarative and make `k6 run` exit 99 when crossed, so pass criteria are code; it is one static binary, so one 4-vCPU container reaches the required rates without distributed workers. Locust would need a custom load shape for open-model arrival and hand-written pass logic.
- **Scripts.** JavaScript ES modules in `tests/load/k6/`, one per scenario (`s1-event-loop.js`, `s2-stateless.js`, `s3-calibrate.js`, `s3-scaleout.js`), each importing only `./lib.js`: the NDJSON stream reader and validator, the token loader, the marker-line printer and the request helper of section 6.4 step 7, whose first `429` response is marked expected through the request's `responseCallback` so it doesn't count in `http_req_failed`, and increments the counter `limit_retries`, which the verdict reports. `selftest.js` exercises `lib.js` against `scripts/ci/k6_selftest_server.py` (T-581).
- **Markers.** Scripts print three marker lines to standard output and never a header or a token: `K6_SUMMARY_JSON <json>` (from `handleSummary`, with every threshold's `ok` and the `limit_retries` count), `K6_FAILED_REQUEST {"traceId", "startedAt", "endedAt", "status", "reason"}` and `K6_SESSION {"sessionId", "traceIds": [...]}`. A stream counts as failed when its status isn't `200`, a line isn't valid JSON or has an unknown `type`, the first event isn't `meta`, `citations` doesn't precede `done`, the stream holds an `error` event, or it doesn't end with exactly one `done`.
- **Requests.** Every request carries a client-generated `traceparent` whose trace ID k6 records. Load users are assigned to virtual users round-robin; each chat virtual user runs sessions of five turns with questions from `tests/fixtures/questions/load.json`; every `cheap` scenario sends `getCurrentUser`, taking its user round-robin from the 20 load users, so no user sends more than 1 request a second (the burst limit admits 10 a second per worker, `11-api.md` section 1.11).
- **The ingestion scenario** (S4) is not an arrival profile; it runs as the Python driver `docchat-load ingest` on `DeployedApiClient`, which refreshes its token during a run of several hours.

### 7.2 Generator

S1 to S3 run from an Azure Container Instances container group per scenario in `rg-docchat-load`, in the environment's region, with no virtual network and no inbound address, created and deleted by the scenario's own job as `id-docchat-citest` (`Microsoft.ContainerInstance/containerGroups@2026-07-01`, template `tests/load/aci/loadgen.bicep`):

- name `ci-docchat-loadgen-<scenario>-<run number>`, `osType: 'Linux'`, `sku: 'Standard'`, `restartPolicy: 'Never'`, one container `k6` with 4 vCPU and 8 GB;
- image: the template parameter `image`, which `docchat-load aci-params` sets to the reference on the final `FROM` line of `apps/ingestion/Dockerfile` (`01-repository.md` section 10), and refuses with exit 2 unless it starts `mcr.microsoft.com/azure-functions/python:` and carries a `@sha256:` digest. The generator pulls nothing from the shared registry (a container-group pull from a network-restricted registry needs trusted services, which the registry keeps off) and nothing from Docker Hub (anonymous pulls from Azure's shared addresses are rate-limited); Microsoft Artifact Registry serves the image the repository already pins and scans;
- command `["python", "/work/run.py"]`, which replaces the image's entrypoint, so the Functions host never starts; environment `K6_URL`, `K6_SHA256`, `K6_MEMBER` (all from the lock), `DOCCHAT_LOAD_TOKENS_FILE=/work/tokens.json` and the scenario's non-secret variables; one secret volume `work`, mounted read-only at `/work`, holding `run.py`, `scenario.js`, `lib.js` and `tokens.json` from `@secure()` parameters.

`tests/load/aci/run.py` uses the Python 3.13 standard library only: it downloads `K6_URL` with `urllib.request.urlopen` (timeout 120 s, 3 attempts 15 s apart), refuses the file unless its SHA-256 equals `K6_SHA256`, reads exactly the member `K6_MEMBER` with `tarfile.extractfile` and writes its bytes to `/tmp/k6` (nothing is extracted by path), sets mode 0755, runs `["/tmp/k6", "run", "--quiet", "/work/scenario.js"]` with inherited standard output, prints `K6_EXIT <k6 exit code>` and exits 0. A download, checksum or extraction failure prints `K6_BOOTSTRAP_FAILED <reason>` (`download_failed`, `checksum_mismatch`, `member_missing`) and exits 0. The container always exits 0 because Container Instances can restart a container that exits nonzero even under `restartPolicy: 'Never'`, which would run the scenario twice; the verdict reads k6's result from the marker, and a log with neither marker fails the scenario (T-579). Pull time doesn't count: a scenario's start T0 is the container's start time. A generator in the same region makes the client-side p95 of 200 ms a measurement of the API rather than of the internet path.

S4 runs on the job's own runner, because its criteria are server-side durations.

### 7.3 Environment

- **`load`** (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 1 and 10.14) has the `prod` SKUs and autoscale rules for every component the test measures (API plan, ingestion plan, SQL, Cosmos DB, AI Search), real storage and Content Safety, and the model stub (section 2.3) as the API's and the ingestion app's model endpoint, with `openAi.enabled: false`. Its resource token includes the workflow run ID, so every run gets new names; it lives at most 48 hours.
- **Dependency capacity.** S3's rate never exceeds what the dependencies that can't be resized per run accept: (1) S3's maximum rate is 60 turns per second; (2) in `load` each of the containers `conversations`, `memories` and `usage` has an autoscale maximum of 30,000 RU/s = 60 turns/s × 400 RU per completed turn × 1.25 headroom, created at that maximum so its physical partitions exist before S1 and no scale-up waits for a split, while `prod` keeps its own maxima, which S1-h checks against S1's measured peak; (3) Prompt Shields S0 accepts 1,000 requests per 10 seconds, so S3's rate is capped at 80 screening calls per second (25% headroom), and each container's demand at its measured RU per turn (section 7.5); (4) the `load` workspace has no daily cap (`monitoring.dailyCapGb` −1), because a full run sends about 6.5 GB (about 1.6 GB for S1, S2, the S3 calibration, the seed, the alert probe and S4, and about 4.9 GB for S3's main phase at 60 turns/s × 900 s × 90 KB per turn), and the run's own volume bounds the cost; (5) every verdict checks that no chat-path Cosmos DB request stayed throttled after the SDK's retries (`C-db`) and that the workspace never reached a daily cap (`C-cap`). Reason: S3 must load the API's CPU, not throttle on a dependency; at `prod`'s 4,000 RU/s and about 300 RU per turn Cosmos DB throttles above about 13 turns per second, and a throttled chat-path call fails after 3 attempts and 5 s (`13-conversations-and-memory.md`), which would fail S3 for a reason unrelated to autoscale.
- **Ops config in `load`**, set by job `seed-config` as the `load` deploy identity: `ratelimit.max_concurrent_streams` 200, `ratelimit.chat_requests_per_minute` 1,200, `quota.tokens_per_day` 1,000,000,000, `quota.tokens_per_month` 100,000,000,000 and `chat.max_turn_seconds` 220; everything else at its default (follow-ups, summaries and memory on), so after-response work loads the system as in production.
- **Stub settings in `load`** (`infra/config/app-settings.json` key `modelStub`, `81-config.md` section 2): `DOCCHAT_STUB_WORKERS=2`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_RECORD=false`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_FIRST_TOKEN_DELAY_MS=800`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKEN_DELAY_MS=25`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_TOKENS_PER_CHUNK=2`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_ANSWER_TOKENS=400`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_TEXT_TOKENS=120`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_JSON_DELAY_MS=300`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_EMBEDDING_DELAY_MS=40`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_EVERY_N=20`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_THROTTLE_DEPLOYMENTS=<ingestion embedding deployment>,<table-summary deployment>`, `DOCCHAT_STUB_RETRY_AFTER_MS=500`.

`chat.max_turn_seconds` 220 holds only while job 5 runs the 200-second stream test; job 6 sets it back to its default 180 before S1, so S2's lease bound of 210 s is the production one.

### 7.4 The workflow `load-test.yaml`

`workflow_dispatch` only, with inputs `deploy_run_id` (the `deploy.yaml` run whose `release-manifest` and digests to test), `scenarios` (`all` or a comma list of `s1`, `s2`, `s3`, `s4`), `rate` (optional integer, turns per second, replacing S3's CPU-derived rate and still capped by its dependency bounds) and `answer_tokens` (integer, default 80, allowed 80 to 4,000); top-level concurrency group `docchat-load-test` with `cancel-in-progress: false`. Jobs that hold test tokens run in GitHub environment `load-tests` as `id-docchat-citest` on the test runner (`runs-on: ${{ vars.DOCCHAT_CI_PRIVATE_NETWORKING == 'true' && fromJSON('{"group":"docchat-ci-private"}') || 'ubuntu-24.04' }}`), and by default end with `firewall.py close --target vault --env load` under `if: always()`; jobs that need the `load` deploy identity run in environment `load` and hold no test token; each signing-in job has `permissions {contents: read, id-token: write}` and runs `az config set extension.use_dynamic_install=no` after signing in. A job of an unselected scenario is skipped. The runbook "Run the load test" is the operator's procedure.

| # | Job | Environment and identity | Timeout | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `manifest` | none; `permissions {contents: read, actions: read}`; no Azure sign-in | 10 min | `python scripts/ci/check_deploy_run.py <deploy_run_id>` fails with `deploy_run_invalid` unless the run's `path` is `.github/workflows/deploy.yaml`, `head_branch` `main` and `conclusion` `success` (T-533); downloads its `release-manifest`; outputs `commit` and the three image references |
| 1 | `create` | calls `deploy-environment.yaml` with `environment: load`, `mode: full` and `resource_token_salt: run-${{ github.run_id }}` (`83-infrastructure.md` section 10.14) | as called | provisions and deploys the release; outputs `api_base_url`, `test_client_id`, `api_scope` |
| 2 | `access-gate` | `load-access` (reviewers `docchat-tenant-admins`) | 10 min after approval | the reviewer completes the gate steps of the runbook "Run the load test" (post-provision identity step for `load`, the Conditional Access exclusion for the run's applications, `testusers check --set load --min-hours 24`) before approving |
| 3 | `seed-config` | `load`, the `load` deploy identity | 20 min | `azd env refresh -e load`; `firewall.py sweep` and `open --target sql --env load`; the five `docchat-ops --env load config set` calls of section 7.3 with `--ticket <run ID>`; `firewall.py close` with `if: always()`; output `applied_at` |
| 4 | `seed` | `load-tests`, `id-docchat-citest` | 90 min | the common steps 1 to 3 of section 7.5; `uv run docchat-load seed`: project `Load test` flagged default, index `load-chat` with the four fixture documents ingested and `completed`, one `getCurrentUser` call per load user, and, from 70 s after `applied_at`, 500 single-turn sessions for `docchat-load-01` |
| 5 | `smoke` | calls `deployed-tests.yaml` with `environment: load`, `suite: smoke` | as called | the `smoke` suite with the `load` alias map, and T-597 |
| 6 | `stream-reset` | `load`, the `load` deploy identity; `if: always()` when `seed-config` ran | 15 min | as `seed-config` with the single call `config set --key chat.max_turn_seconds --value 180 --reason "load stream test reset"`; output `applied_at` |
| 7 | `s1`, `s2`, `s3` | `load-tests`, `id-docchat-citest`, one after another, `s1` starting no sooner than 70 s after `stream-reset`'s `applied_at` | 60, 60 and 100 min | section 7.5 for each; `s3` also runs the alert probe |
| 8 | `s4-set1-config` | `load`, the `load` deploy identity | 15 min | as `seed-config` with the single call `config set --key ingestion.max_parallel_items --value 1 --reason "load S4 set 1"`; output `applied_at` |
| 9 | `s4-set1` | `load-tests`, `id-docchat-citest` | 350 min | S4 set 1 (variant `a`), starting no sooner than 70 s after `applied_at` |
| 10 | `s4-set2-config` | as job 8 | 15 min | `--value 4 --reason "load S4 set 2"` |
| 11 | `s4-set2` | `load-tests`, `id-docchat-citest`, `permissions {contents: read, id-token: write, actions: read}` | 350 min | S4 set 2 (variant `b`) with set 1's artifact for S4-b |
| 12 | `s4-reset` | as job 8; `if: always()` when `s4-set2-config` ran | 15 min | `--value 1 --reason "load S4 reset"` |
| 13 | `verdict` | none; no Azure sign-in | 10 min | downloads every `load-results/<name>/verdict.json`, writes the table to the job summary, and fails when any selected scenario or the alert probe failed or came out `inconclusive` |
| 14 | `teardown` | `load`, the `load` deploy identity, concurrency group `docchat-env-load`; `if: always()` | 60 min | `uv run --frozen python scripts/deploy/teardown_load.py` (`83-infrastructure.md` section 10.14) |

A run whose `teardown` never ran is removed by `nightly.yaml`'s `load-guard` job (`83-infrastructure.md` section 10.14).

### 7.5 Scenario run flow

**Common steps of every scenario job.** The job's variables are `API_BASE_URL`, `TEST_CLIENT_ID` and `API_SCOPE` from `create`'s outputs and `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID`, `AZURE_LOCATION`, `AZURE_RESOURCE_GROUP` (`rg-docchat-load`), `DOCCHAT_CI_TEST_CLIENT_ID`, `DOCCHAT_TEST_VAULT_URI` and `DOCCHAT_TEST_USER_DOMAIN` from the `load-tests` variables; every other value comes from `.azure/load/.env`, written in step 2. The job never signs in as a deploy identity: its Azure rights are `id-docchat-citest`'s DocChat Load Test Operator (deployments, container groups, autoscale writes) and Log Analytics Reader (every read) at `rg-docchat-load` (`83-infrastructure.md` section 6.8).

1. Checkout; setup-uv; `uv sync --locked --package docchat-testing --package docchat-ops`; for S1 to S3, k6 from `tests/load/k6.lock.json` into `$RUNNER_TEMP/k6`, its SHA-256 verified.
2. `azure/login` as `id-docchat-citest`; `az config set extension.use_dynamic_install=no`; `azd auth login --client-id <DOCCHAT_CI_TEST_CLIENT_ID> --federated-credential-provider github --tenant-id <AZURE_TENANT_ID>`; `azd env new load --subscription <AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID> --location <AZURE_LOCATION> --no-prompt`; `azd env set AZURE_RESOURCE_GROUP rg-docchat-load`; `azd env refresh -e load --no-prompt`.
3. For S1 to S3 and `seed`: `uv run docchat-load tokens --env load --aliases load-01..load-20,load-admin --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/tokens.json"` (section 6.4). The S4 jobs read their one password when their driver starts.
4. The scenario's setup. The autoscale setting's name is `az monitor autoscale list --resource-group rg-docchat-load --query "[?targetResourceUri=='<API plan ID>'].name" -o tsv`, the plan ID `az webapp show --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name <DOCCHAT_API_APP_NAME> --query serverFarmId -o tsv`; every capacity change is `az monitor autoscale update --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name <setting> --min-count <n> --max-count <n> --count <n>`, after which `az webapp list-instances --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name <DOCCHAT_API_APP_NAME> --query "length(@)" -o tsv` is polled every 30 s until it equals the target (15 minutes, then exit 1).
5. `"$RUNNER_TEMP/k6/k6" inspect tests/load/k6/<scenario>.js > /dev/null`; `uv run docchat-load aci-params --scenario <id> --bootstrap tests/load/aci/run.py --script tests/load/k6/<scenario>.js --lib tests/load/k6/lib.js --tokens "$RUNNER_TEMP/tokens.json" --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/loadgen.parameters.json"` (mode 0600).
6. `az deployment group create --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name loadgen-<id>-<run number> --template-file tests/load/aci/loadgen.bicep --parameters @"$RUNNER_TEMP/loadgen.parameters.json"`; the parameters file is deleted.
7. Every 15 s, `az container show --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name ci-docchat-loadgen-<id>-<run number> --query "{state: instanceView.state, started: containers[0].instanceView.currentState.startTime}" -o json` until the state is `Succeeded` or `Failed` (scenario length + 15 minutes, then exit 1); `started` is T0.
8. `az container logs … > load-results/<id>/k6.log`; then, with `if: always()`, `az container delete --resource-group rg-docchat-load --name <group> --yes`.
9. `uv run docchat-load verdict --scenario <id> --k6-log load-results/<id>/k6.log --start <T0> --end <T0 + length> --run-start <the docchat-load-created tag> --out load-results/<id>/verdict.json`: fails when the log holds `K6_BOOTSTRAP_FAILED` or no `K6_EXIT` line; records k6's exit code (0 every threshold passed, 99 a threshold failed, any other code a k6 error) and `limit_retries`; runs the scenario's KQL through the Log Analytics query API (`docchat_testing.logquery.query_logs`, workspace `DOCCHAT_LOG_ANALYTICS_WORKSPACE_ID`, timespan `<start>/<end>`, its token from the job's Azure CLI sign-in), platform metrics through `az monitor metrics list` and the Activity Log through `az monitor activity-log list --resource-group rg-docchat-load`; first waits until telemetry of the scenario's last minute has arrived (every 30 s, at most 10 minutes); writes the criteria table to the job summary; exits 1 on any failed criterion, and 0 with `inconclusive: true` and its `cause` for S3's `calibration_miss` or `dependency_bound`.
10. Uploads `load-results/<id>/`; restores the `load` profile (`--min-count 1 --max-count 6 --count 1`) with `if: always()`; by default, last, `uv run --frozen python scripts/deploy/firewall.py close --target vault --env load` with `if: always()` (the vault's read window itself opened and closed inside `docchat-load tokens` or the S4 driver's password read, section 6.4).

**S1 setup:** capacity 1. Run `s1-event-loop` (10 minutes).

**S2 setup:** capacity 3. Start `s2-stateless` (9 minutes); at T0 + 240 s set capacity 2 (minimum = maximum = default = 2); poll `az webapp list-instances … --query "length(@)"` every 5 s; T_r is the first poll returning 2. The removed instance is the `AppRoleInstance` with `AppRequests` rows in [T_r − 120 s, T_r] and none in [T_r + 60 s, T0 + 9 min]. After the verdict, capacity 1 is restored.

**S3 setup and calibration.** Setup: the `load` profile (minimum 1, default 1, maximum 6, with `prod`'s scale-out rule: `CpuPercentage` average above 70 over 5 minutes, +1 instance, cooldown 5 minutes), and 1 instance before the start; N0 = 1. L = the input `answer_tokens` and d = max(1, floor(800 ÷ L)) ms, passed to both scripts as `DOCCHAT_LOAD_ANSWER_TOKENS` and `DOCCHAT_LOAD_TOKEN_DELAY_MS`, which append `[[stub:tokens=<L>]] [[stub:delay_ms=<d>]]` to every question.

1. Calibrate: run `s3-calibrate` (4 minutes). Then `uv run docchat-load s3-rate --start <T0> --answer-tokens <L> [--rate <input>] --out load-results/s3/rate.json` waits until the metrics and logs of calibration minutes 2 to 4 have arrived (every 30 s, at most 10 minutes) and reads: C5, the mean of `az monitor metrics list --resource <API plan ID> --metric CpuPercentage --interval PT1M --aggregation Average --start-time <T0 + 1 min> --end-time <T0 + 4 min>`; s, the mean Prompt Shields calls per turn, `AppDependencies | where TimeGenerated between (<T0 + 1 min> .. <T0 + 4 min>) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and Name == "docchat.chat.screen" | summarize s = avg(todouble(Properties["docchat.calls"]))`; ru[c], S1-h's `ruPerTurn` over the same window; T[c], `az cosmosdb sql container throughput show --account-name <DOCCHAT_COSMOS_ACCOUNT_NAME> --resource-group rg-docchat-load --database-name docchat --name <c> --query resource.autoscaleSettings.maxThroughput -o tsv` for `conversations`, `memories` and `usage`. It writes R = min(R_cpu, R_ps, R_db, 60) turns/s with R_cpu = ceil(5 × 85 ÷ C5) (the input `rate` in its place when given), R_ps = floor(80 ÷ s) and R_db = the smallest floor(T[c] ÷ (1.25 × ru[c])); every value goes into the verdict's `measurements` (T-583). R below 5, the calibration rate, ends S3 before the main phase as `inconclusive` with cause `dependency_bound`.
2. Run `s3-scaleout` (15 minutes) with `DOCCHAT_LOAD_RATE=R` and the same L; T_main is its T0.
3. **Inconclusive runs.** When only S3-a or S3-b fails and every 1-minute CPU average of the main phase is below 70, the verdict is `inconclusive`: cause `calibration_miss` when R = R_cpu, and the next run sets `rate` = ceil(1.25 × R); cause `dependency_bound` when R < R_cpu, and the next run sets `answer_tokens` to four times the last value (80, 320, 1,280, then 4,000), which raises the API's CPU per turn while each turn's dependency calls stay the same. A `dependency_bound` verdict at 4,000 goes to the product owner with its measurements (runbook "Run the load test" step 12).

**The alert probe** (job `s3`; validates alerts A41 and A42 of `85-observability.md` section 6.3). Before S3's setup, `uv run docchat-load probe-alerts --phase send --tokens "$RUNNER_TEMP/tokens.json" --out load-results/alert-probe/` sends, as `docchat-load-01`, 25 `createChatTurn` requests one after another in one new session, each a `load.json` question followed by `[[stub:first_ms=12000@stream]] [[stub:first_ms=9000@json]]`, and records the window from the first request to 60 s after the last `done`. Each answer's first chunk then arrives after 12 s (A41's signal), and each structured call waits 9 s, so the 25 memory extractions, within their 20-second timeout, average about 9,000 ms (A42's signal); the rewrite and follow-up calls time out and are excluded by A42's `outcome` filter. After S3's verdict, `--phase check` runs A41's and A42's queries over the recorded window with the thresholds 10,000 ms and 8,000 ms and writes `load-results/alert-probe/verdict.json`.

**S4 sets.** Each ingest job runs the common steps 1 and 2, then:

1. Waits until 70 s after its config job's `applied_at` (the ops-config cache lifetime of 60 s plus margin).
2. `uv run docchat-load build-corpus --variant <a|b> --count 200 --out "$RUNNER_TEMP/corpus"` (T-578): the corpus of section 1.5, every file byte-unique.
3. `uv run docchat-load ingest --env load --variant <a|b> --index load-ingest-<a|b> --corpus "$RUNNER_TEMP/corpus" --out load-results/s4-set<n>/`: reads the `load-admin` password from the test vault (by default inside its own read window, `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.16; under the private-networking option through the vault's private endpoint) and keeps it in memory; creates the index with the page-wise method at its default parameters and maps it to project `Load test`; preflights in batches of 50; reserves and uploads with 8 requests in flight through `DeployedApiClient`; submits two `adminSubmitUploads` calls of 100 versions within 5 s; polls `adminGetIngestionRun` for both runs every 30 s until both are terminal or 330 minutes pass; re-acquires the admin token within 5 minutes of its expiry; writes `{runs: [{runId, state, createdAt, completedAt, counts}], setStart, setEnd}`.
4. Set 2 only: downloads set 1's artifact.
5. The verdict of section 7.6.

**S5** is not a load profile: it is the Cosmos DB data-access audit (T-301) and the plan integration test (T-067) passing on the release commit, read from that commit's `python-tests` job.

### 7.6 Scenarios, arrival profiles and pass criteria

Every criterion is computed by `docchat-load verdict` or `docchat-load probe-alerts --phase check` from the sources named; all criteria of a scenario must hold. `s` and `e` are the scenario window's start and end.

| Scenario | Target | Setup | Arrival profile | Pass criteria |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 (event loop) | 100 concurrent streams on one instance of the smallest production size with zero errors and the cheap endpoint's p95 at most 200 ms | capacity 1 (one P1v3 instance, 2 Gunicorn workers) | `streams`: ramping-vus 0 → 100 over 60 s, hold 9 minutes, graceful ramp-down 30 s (each stream about 11 s: 800 ms + 400 × 25 ms plus pipeline time); `cheap`: constant-arrival-rate 20 requests/s of `getCurrentUser` from minute 1 to minute 10, 20 to 60 VUs; `sessions`: constant-arrival-rate 1 request/s of `listSessions` as `docchat-load-01` (500 sessions) from minute 2 to minute 9 | S1-a `http_req_failed{scenario:streams}` rate = 0; S1-b `checks{scenario:streams}` rate = 1 (status `200`, first event `meta`, `citations` before `done`, exactly one terminal event and it is `done`, every line valid JSON with a known `type`); S1-c `http_req_duration{scenario:cheap}` p(95) ≤ 200 ms; S1-d `http_req_failed{scenario:cheap}` rate = 0; S1-e the per-minute maximum of concurrent `POST /v1/chat/turns` requests is ≥ 95 in every minute from 2 to 9; S1-f zero `5xx` in `AppRequests`; S1-g exactly one `AppRoleInstance` served the run; S1-h every container's request charge per completed turn ≤ 400 RU and its highest 1-minute consumption ÷ 60 s ≤ that container's `prod` autoscale maximum in `infra/env/prod.json` at the release commit; S1-i the p95 over completed turns of the request charge summed over each turn's trace ≤ 400 RU; S1-j `http_req_duration{scenario:sessions}` p(95) ≤ 200 ms and `http_req_failed{scenario:sessions}` rate = 0; S1-k every turn completed in the window has its memory extraction run within 5 minutes of the window's end; `C-db`; `C-cap` |
| S2 (statelessness) | any instance serves any request; stopping one instance fails only the streams it held | capacity 3 | `streams`: ramping-vus 0 → 60 over 60 s, hold 8 minutes; `cheap`: 10 requests/s; capacity 2 at T0 + 240 s | S2-a every `K6_FAILED_REQUEST` either has its `AppRequests` row (`OperationId` = its trace ID) on the removed instance, or has no row and started before T_r + 5 s; S2-b `AppRequests | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and AppRoleInstance != "<removed>" and (Success == false or toint(ResultCode) >= 500) | count` returns 0 and no `K6_FAILED_REQUEST` maps to a surviving instance; S2-c no `K6_FAILED_REQUEST` started at or after T_r + 60 s; S2-d at least 30% of the `K6_SESSION` records with two or more turns map their trace IDs to two or more `AppRoleInstance` values, and none of their trace IDs is a `K6_FAILED_REQUEST`; S2-e at T_r + 210 s, for every session holding a failed stream, `getSession` shows that turn `failed` with `errorCode` `service_restarting` (its drain reached it) or `turn_abandoned` (cut without its finaliser), never `streaming`, and a new turn sent in that session with a new `clientTurnId` is accepted and ends with `done`; `C-db`; `C-cap` |
| S3 (scale-out) | autoscale adds an instance when average CPU stays above 70% for 5 minutes, with the cheap endpoint's p95 at most 200 ms meanwhile | the `load` profile, 1 instance at the start | calibration: constant-arrival-rate 5 turns/s with `[[stub:tokens=<L>]] [[stub:delay_ms=<d>]]` plus 10 requests/s cheap, 4 minutes; main: constant-arrival-rate R turns/s (2R to 6R VUs) with the same L, plus 20 requests/s cheap, 15 minutes | S3-a the earliest `eventTimestamp` of `az monitor activity-log list --resource-group rg-docchat-load --start-time <T_main> --end-time <T_main + 20 min> --query "[?operationName.value=='Microsoft.Insights/AutoscaleSettings/Scaleup/Action' && status.value=='Succeeded'].eventTimestamp" -o tsv` lies in [T_main + 5 min, T_main + 12 min]; S3-b the mean of the five 1-minute plan `CpuPercentage` averages ending at that time is > 70; S3-c the instance count at the end ≥ N0 + 1; S3-d `http_req_duration{scenario:cheap}` p(95) ≤ 200 ms over the main phase; S3-e `http_req_failed` rate = 0 in both scenarios; `C-db`; `C-cap` |
| S4 (ingestion) | 200 documents at parallelism 1 with no throttling failure; at parallelism 4 at most half the time, throttled calls retried without failing a document | ingestion always-ready instances 2 (two EP2 instances); the stub throttles every 20th ingestion model request | set 1: `ingestion.max_parallel_items` 1, variant `a` into `load-ingest-a`; set 2: parallelism 4, variant `b` into `load-ingest-b`; each set: preflight in batches of 50, upload with 8 in flight, two `adminSubmitUploads` calls of 100 versions within 5 s | S4-a both runs of each set end `completed` with `counts.succeeded` = 100 and `counts.failed` = 0; S4-b duration(set 2) ≤ 0.5 × duration(set 1), duration being the latest `completedAt` minus the earliest `createdAt` of the set's runs; S4-c `AppMetrics | where TimeGenerated between (setStart .. setEnd) and Name == "docchat.ingestion.throttled" | summarize n = sum(Sum)` > 0 for each set; `C-cap` |
| S5 (data access) | chat-path Cosmos DB queries single-partition; request-path SQL index-backed | the CI integration environment | none | T-301 and T-067 pass on the release commit |
| Alert probe | alerts A41 and A42 fire on their signals | before S3's setup | 25 sequential turns of section 7.5 | P-A41 A41's query returns a row for the chat deployment over the probe's window; P-A42 A42's query returns a row with `call` = `memory`; `C-cap` |

**Common criteria.** `C-db` (S1 to S3): no chat-path Cosmos DB request stayed throttled after the SDK's retries:

```kusto
AppMetrics
| where TimeGenerated between (s .. e)
| where (Name == "docchat.api.problems" and tostring(Properties["code"]) == "dependency_unavailable")
     or (Name == "docchat.chat.turns" and tostring(Properties["error_code"]) in ("conversation_unavailable", "turn_save_failed"))
     or Name == "docchat.usage.record_failures"
| summarize n = sum(Sum)
```

passes when `n` is 0 or no row returns; the verdict reports `CDBDataPlaneRequests | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and DatabaseName == "docchat" | summarize throttled = countif(StatusCode == 429) by CollectionName` (throttling the SDK absorbed), and names the cause `dependency_capacity` when `C-db` fails with that count above 0. `C-cap` (every verdict): `_LogOperation | where TimeGenerated between (<run start> .. now()) | where Category =~ "Ingestion" | where Detail contains "OverQuota" | count` returns 0, so no criterion was computed from telemetry a daily cap had cut.

**S1-e:**

```kusto
let s = datetime(<T0>); let e = datetime(<T0 + 10m>);
let r = AppRequests
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and Name == "POST /v1/chat/turns"
  | project st = TimeGenerated, en = TimeGenerated + DurationMs * 1ms;
union (r | project t = st, d = 1), (r | project t = en, d = -1)
| order by t asc
| extend concurrent = row_cumsum(d)
| summarize peak = max(concurrent) by minute = bin(t, 1m)
| where minute between ((s + 2m) .. (s + 9m))
| summarize minPeak = min(peak)
```

passes when `minPeak` ≥ 95. S1-f: `AppRequests | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and toint(ResultCode) >= 500 | summarize n = sum(ItemCount)` returns 0. S1-g: the same filter without the result code, `summarize instances = dcount(AppRoleInstance)`, returns 1.

**S1-h:**

```kusto
let s = datetime(<T0>); let e = datetime(<T0 + 10m>);
let turns = toscalar(AppRequests
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and Name == "POST /v1/chat/turns" and ResultCode == "200"
  | summarize sum(ItemCount));
CDBDataPlaneRequests
| where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and DatabaseName == "docchat"
| summarize ru = sum(RequestCharge), throttled = countif(StatusCode == 429) by CollectionName, minute = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| summarize ruPerTurn = sum(ru) / turns, peakRuPerSecond = max(ru) / 60.0, throttled = sum(throttled) by CollectionName
```

**S1-i and S1-k:**

```kusto
let s = datetime(<T0>); let e = datetime(<T0 + 10m>);
let done = AppDependencies
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api"
      and Name == "docchat.conversations.finalize_turn" and tostring(Properties["docchat.turn.status"]) == "completed"
  | distinct OperationId;
AppDependencies
| where TimeGenerated between (s .. e + 5m) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api" and isnotnull(Properties["docchat.cosmos.request_charge"])
| join kind=inner done on OperationId
| summarize ru = sum(todouble(Properties["docchat.cosmos.request_charge"])) by OperationId
| summarize p95 = percentile(ru, 95), turns = count()
```

S1-i passes when `p95` ≤ 400.

```kusto
let s = datetime(<T0>); let e = datetime(<T0 + 10m>);
let started = AppDependencies
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and AppRoleName == "docchat-api"
      and Name == "docchat.conversations.begin_turn" and tostring(Properties["docchat.outcome"]) == "started"
  | project OperationId, turn = tostring(Properties["docchat.turn_id"]);
let done = AppDependencies
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e) and Name == "docchat.conversations.finalize_turn"
      and tostring(Properties["docchat.turn.status"]) == "completed"
  | distinct OperationId;
let extracted = AppDependencies
  | where TimeGenerated between (s .. e + 5m) and Name == "docchat.memory.extract"
  | distinct turn = tostring(Properties["docchat.turn_id"]);
started | join kind=inner done on OperationId | join kind=leftanti extracted on turn | count
```

S1-k passes when the count is 0; the verdict waits until 10 minutes after `e` before running it, so that extractions started in the last 5 minutes have arrived.

### 7.7 Request-unit measurement

The emulator has no request-unit accounting, so request units are measured only in `load`: S1-h per container from `CDBDataPlaneRequests` (the platform's own charges, mean per completed turn), S1-i per completed turn from the `docchat.cosmos.request_charge` attributes of the turn's spans (`85-observability.md` section 4), whose p95 is the figure `13-conversations-and-memory.md` acceptance criterion 49 bounds. `load`'s containers are sized from the 400 RU bound (section 7.3), and the verdict records `ruPerTurn`, `peakRuPerSecond` and `throttled` per container and S1-i's `p95` in `measurements`, so a release that raises the charge is visible before it reaches `prod`'s sizing.

### 7.8 Scaling targets and their criteria

| Target | Proven by |
|---|---|
| Event loop: 100 concurrent streams on one instance of the smallest production size, zero errors, cheap endpoint p95 ≤ 200 ms | S1-a to S1-g (T-591), with the loop guard of section 2.8 in every integration test |
| Statelessness: any instance serves any request; stopping one fails only its streams | S2-a to S2-e (T-592) |
| Scale-out: an instance is added when CPU stays above 70% for 5 minutes, cheap p95 ≤ 200 ms meanwhile | S3-a to S3-e (T-593) |
| Ingestion: 200 documents at parallelism 1 without throttling failures, at most half the time at parallelism 4 | S4-a to S4-c (T-594) |
| Data access: chat-path Cosmos DB queries single-partition; request-path SQL index-backed | S5 (T-595) |
| Cosmos DB sizing: at most 400 RU per completed turn; `prod`'s maxima carry 100 streams | S1-h, S1-i |
| Session list latency with 500 sessions under load | S1-j |
| After-response work keeps up | S1-k |
| Latency alerts fire on their signals | P-A41, P-A42 (T-596) |
| No verdict computed from capped or throttled data | `C-db`, `C-cap` (T-584) |

### 7.9 When the load test runs

All scenarios and the alert probe run at the acceptance of the load-test stage named in `90-build-order.md`. S1 to S4 also run before any `prod` deploy that changes the API or ingestion plan SKU, the Gunicorn worker settings, the Durable concurrency settings, the AI Search tier or replica count, or adds a model call to the chat path; the release checklist (`86-operations.md`) names these triggers, and the verdicts are attached to the `prod` deploy approval.

## 8. Infrastructure checks

### 8.1 The pull-request gate

Three jobs of `ci.yaml` gate every pull request on infrastructure; each is a required check.

- **`infra-validate`** and **`preflight-whatif`** are `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.5's jobs. What must pass: `bicep build` of `infra/main.bicep` and `infra/shared.bicep` and `bicep build-params` of `infra/main.bicepparam` for `dev`, `prod` and `load` with Bicep CLI 0.47.16 (T-421); `bicep lint` with every analyzer rule at `error` (same row); `check_bicep_modules.py` (T-422); PSRule (microsoft/ps-rule v2.9.0, PSRule.Rules.Azure 1.47.0 with `requires: '>=1.47.0 <1.48.0'`, baseline `Azure.GA_2025_12`, all confirmed 2026-10-14) for the three expansions with failures allowed only through suppression groups carrying `spec.expiresOn` and a `# Synopsis:` reason, an expired suppression being an error (T-423); the preflight for `dev` (T-434); what-if under `id-docchat-pr` with `--validation-level ProviderNoRbac` against `rg-docchat-dev` and `rg-docchat-shared` (T-431); and the refused deployment write as `id-docchat-pr`, step `pr-write-refused` (T-432). A PSRule module release outside the range fails every run until the runbook "Dependency, base-image and tool updates" moves the range and the baseline together, so the gate fails closed. Fork pull requests get no OIDC token, so a maintainer re-runs the change on a same-repository branch before merge. Microsoft Graph resources, which what-if can't evaluate, change only with code-owner review (`01-repository.md` section 2.6).
- **`infra-tests`** (no GitHub environment, no Azure sign-in, `permissions {contents: read}`, `ubuntu-24.04`, 20 minutes): checkout; setup-uv; Bicep CLI 0.47.16 (`az bicep install --version v0.47.16`); `az bicep build` of `main.bicep` and `shared.bicep`, the bootstrap templates and `build-params` for each environment into `build/infra/`; then `uv run pytest tests/infra --compiled build/infra`. Its tests read the compiled JSON, so they run in seconds and need no Azure account:

| Test | What it checks in the compiled templates |
|---|---|
| T-125 | the typed environment files: a negative fixture per kind (undeclared member, removed member, mistyped field, extra nested field such as `openAi.deployments.chat`) fails `build-params` whichever environment it targets |
| T-424 | subnets, delegations and every network security rule of `83-infrastructure.md` section 4.3; 13 private endpoints and 10 private DNS zones per environment |
| T-425 | every role assignment against the call matrices of `83-infrastructure.md` section 6 |
| T-426 | every diagnostic setting's categories against `83-infrastructure.md` section 2.19 |
| T-427, T-428 | container time-to-live, workspace table retention and the backup policies |
| T-429, T-430 | the test vault's network settings; the Cosmos DB module's API version and its missing `diagnosticLogSettings` |
| T-126, T-127 | the Azure OpenAI deployment types and the embedding catalogue setting (`81-config.md`) |
| T-500, T-498, T-502 | the alert rules, workbooks and security alerts of `85-observability.md` |

`tests/infra/` sits outside the pytest `testpaths`, so the `python-tests` job doesn't collect it.

### 8.2 The deployed infrastructure suite

`tests/deployed/test_infrastructure.py`, marker `infra`, runs as job `infra-checks` of `deployed-tests.yaml` (section 6.3) in `dev` as the `dev` deploy identity, weekly, after every change under `infra/` reaches `dev`, and on dispatch. It needs no test user. Each test that needs SQL opens the runner rule with `firewall.py open --target sql --env dev` and closes it in a `finally` block; each test that changes a setting restores it before it ends, and the next `dev` deploy restores anything a failed test left.

| T-443 | the Deny policies refuse local authentication, public blob access and federated credentials on app identities |

| T-447 | no publishing surface on the API |
| T-144 | the ingestion app refuses every inbound request |
| T-474 | TLS 1.3 only on the API |

These rows are steps of `83-infrastructure.md` section 10's workflows, and fail their job: T-437 after each provision; T-445 after each `dev` deploy; T-452 and T-469 in `prod`; T-454 and T-453 in every deploy; T-114 (step `app-settings-check`) after every deploy in `dev`, `prod` and `load`; T-081 (step `grants-check`) after every `dev` deploy; T-463 after each web upload; T-475 after each provision; T-124 in every `postprovision` hook; T-472 after a second provision of each environment; T-473 in each environment with scanning on, before uploads open (runbook "Defender acceptance check").
