Performed by the person the stage or runbook names, and recorded: the reading of T-440 at stage 0 (section 6.3); T-471 and T-459 at the acceptance of the stage that provisions `dev`; T-455, T-464, T-465 and T-466 as rehearsals of the runbooks "Rollback" and "Reset the Durable task hub"; T-468 in every load run.

## 9. Security scans and thresholds

### 9.1 The rule

Every finding at high or critical severity fails the build. No waiver, label or setting turns that off; waivers exist only for findings below that threshold. A high or critical finding with no fix blocks merges and releases until the base image's digest moves, the package leaves the final image stage, or the dependency that brings it is replaced or dropped; when none of these is possible, the product owner decides through an ADR (runbook "Dependency, base-image and tool updates"). There are two such decisions: ADR 0153's named exception for Trivy's Dockerfile check DS002 on the ingestion image, whose Functions host keeps UID 0 for the parsing child's user switch, and ADR 0154's exception for a HIGH or CRITICAL vulnerability of an OS package in an image's own Trivy report for which the report names no fix, at most 90 days at a time. Section 9.3 gives their forms and limits; no other high or critical finding has an exception, and a finding with a fix is never excepted.

### 9.2 Scanners

| Scanner and version (confirmed 2026-10-14) | Where and how | Fails the build, never waivable | Fails unless waived |
|---|---|---|---|
| CodeQL, query suite `security-extended`; languages `python`, `javascript-typescript`, `actions` | `codeql.yaml` on pull requests, pushes to `main` and Mondays at 03:00 UTC | merge protection: any open alert of security severity high or critical, and any alert of severity error | none; the only dismissal of a high or critical alert is the reason `false positive`, given by a handle listed in `.github/security/approvers.yaml` (section 9.4) |
| pip-audit 2.10.1 | `security` job: `uv export --locked --all-packages --all-groups --no-emit-workspace --format requirements-txt -o build/audit/requirements.txt`; `uv run pip-audit --strict --disable-pip --require-hashes -r build/audit/requirements.txt --vulnerability-service osv --format json -o build/audit/pip-audit.json \|\| test $? -eq 1` | any finding rated `HIGH` or `CRITICAL`, and any finding no record rates | `LOW` and `MODERATE` |
| npm audit | `security` job, in `apps/web`, `tests/e2e` and `scripts/deploy/swa-cli`: `npm audit --json > build/audit/npm-<dir>.json \|\| test $? -eq 1` | advisories `high` or `critical` | `low` and `moderate` |
| Trivy 0.76.0 | `images` job and `nightly.yaml` `trivy-rescan`: `trivy image --input <tar> --scanners vuln,secret --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --format json --exit-code 0 --output build/audit/trivy-<image>.json` per image; `trivy config --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --format json --exit-code 0 --output build/audit/trivy-config.json` on the three Dockerfiles | any HIGH or CRITICAL vulnerability (unfixed included) but an OS-package vulnerability without a fix in an image's own report while that image's `unfixed-os-packages` exception of section 9.3 is valid, any secret in any layer, any HIGH or CRITICAL Dockerfile misconfiguration but DS002 on the ingestion image while its exception of section 9.3 is valid | none |
| gitleaks 8.30.1 | `security` job on each pull request, from the image pinned in `01-repository.md` section 5.7: `gitleaks git --redact --log-opts="<base>..<head>"`; `nightly.yaml` over the full history; the pre-commit hook of `01-repository.md` section 5.7 locally | any finding: the credential is revoked and the history rewritten (runbook "Incident response") | none |
| zizmor 1.30.1 | `security` job: `uv run zizmor --format json --min-severity medium --no-exit-codes .github/workflows > build/audit/zizmor.json` | `high` | `medium` |
| deptry 0.25.1 | `security` job, per member: `uv run deptry <member path> --json-output build/audit/deptry-<member>.json \|\| test $? -eq 1`; no `[tool.deptry]` ignore is configured but `docchat-ingestion`'s `DEP002` for `azure-functions-runtime` and `opencv-python-headless` and `docchat-model-stub`'s `DEP002` for `uvicorn` (`01-repository.md` section 3.3) | none | any unused, missing or transitive-only dependency |

Exit code 1 of pip-audit, npm audit and deptry means findings were reported, which the gate judges; any other nonzero exit code fails the step as a tool failure. Third-party images used only by local test tooling (the compose emulators) are pinned by digest and kept current by Dependabot without the gate; every image this repository builds or runs in Azure, the load generator's included, is scanned.

**Severity of a pip-audit finding.** pip-audit reports identifiers without severities, so the gate reads each identifier's record from the OSV API (`GET https://api.osv.dev/v1/vulns/<id>`, `urllib.request.urlopen` with a 15-second timeout, 3 attempts 15 s apart) and, when that record has no `database_specific.severity`, the record of each `GHSA-` identifier in its `aliases`; the rating is the highest `database_specific.severity` found. A finding no record rates, or whose records can't be fetched, fails like `HIGH`.

### 9.3 The gate and waivers

`scripts/ci/security_gate.py` (Python standard library) reads every report of `build/audit/`, `.github/security/waivers.yaml`, `.github/security/approvers.yaml` and `.github/security/exceptions.yaml`, and fails when a report is missing or isn't a valid report of its scanner, when any never-waivable finding exists that the exceptions below don't cover (a waiver naming one is reported as not applicable and changes nothing), when a lower finding has no matching waiver, or when any waiver or exception is invalid; it writes the findings-and-waivers table to the job summary (T-527). The build job of `deploy.yaml` runs the same gate on its Trivy output before pushing, so an image with a high or critical finding is never pushed.

A waiver has `id` (GHSA, PYSEC or OSV identifier, npm advisory identifier, zizmor rule identifier or deptry rule code), `scanner` (`pip-audit` | `npm-audit` | `zizmor` | `deptry`), `scope` (package or path glob), `reason` (at least 20 characters), `ticket` (URL), `added` (date), `expires` (date, at most `added` + 90 days) and `approved_by` (a handle listed in `approvers.yaml`). `approvers.yaml` holds `approvers: [<GitHub handle>]`, the members of `docchat-security`, kept equal to the team by the runbook "Repository and GitHub settings"; the gate reads the list from the file because a workflow token can't read organisation team membership. Both files sit under `/.github/security/`, which only `docchat-security` code owners approve. The nightly run warns 14 days before a waiver expires.

**The exceptions at high severity** (ADR 0153, ADR 0154). `.github/security/exceptions.yaml`, beside the waivers and read with the same YAML subset, holds `exceptions:` and entries with exactly `id`, `image`, `reason` (at least 20 characters), `decision` (the ADR that records the product owner's decision, written `ADR NNNN`), `added` and `review_by` (dates, `YYYY-MM-DD`). The gate admits two kinds of entry, held in its code rather than the file, and an entry that names any other `id` or `image` is invalid:

- **`DS002` on `ingestion`** (ADR 0153): Trivy's Dockerfile check "Image user should not be 'root'", which the ingestion image raises because its Functions host keeps UID 0 for the parsing child's user switch (`14-ingestion.md` section 15.6; ADR 0049). A valid entry passes a HIGH DS002 misconfiguration in `trivy-ingestion.json`, or in `trivy-config.json` on the target `apps/ingestion/Dockerfile` (where `trivy config` reports it), and nothing else: never DS002 in another image's report or on another Dockerfile, and never another check, a CRITICAL rating, a vulnerability or a secret of the ingestion image. `review_by` is at most 184 days (six calendar months) after `added`; the first entry was added on 2026-11-06 with `review_by` 2027-05-06.
- **`unfixed-os-packages` on `api`, `ingestion` or `model-stub`** (ADR 0154), one entry per image: a valid entry passes a vulnerability in that image's own report `trivy-<image>.json` only when its result's `Class` is `os-pkgs` (a package of the base image's distribution), it is rated HIGH or CRITICAL, and the report gives it no `FixedVersion` and no `Status` `fixed`. A language-package finding (`lang-pkgs`: a Python distribution, the Functions host's .NET runtime, a Go module), a finding with a fix, a secret, a misconfiguration, a finding in `trivy-config.json` and another image's finding still fail. `review_by` is at most 90 days after `added`; the first entries were added on 2026-11-07 with `review_by` 2027-02-05. Every runtime stage takes the Debian security updates its base digest predates (`01-repository.md` section 4.1 rule 3), so a fixed package doesn't reach the report; and because nightly `trivy-rescan` passes the deployed images' reports through the same gate, a finding whose report gains a fixed version fails the next night.

An entry with a missing, unknown, repeated or malformed field, an `added` after today or a `review_by` beyond its kind's limit is invalid and fails the gate, as does a missing file; from the day after `review_by` the entry is invalid too, so every gate run fails, the nightly runs included, until `docchat-security` renews it with new dates or removes it. The gate's summary and the nightly run warn 14 days before each `review_by`. `scripts/ci/` is a `docchat-security` code-owner path too, so widening an exception takes a reviewed change to the gate, this section and a new ADR.

### 9.4 CodeQL dismissals

`scripts/ci/check_codeql_dismissals.py`, run by `nightly.yaml` job `codeql-dismissals` (`permissions {contents: read, security-events: read, issues: write}`), lists every dismissed alert (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/code-scanning/alerts?state=dismissed&per_page=100`, all pages) and fails when an alert whose `rule.security_severity_level` is `high` or `critical` has a `dismissed_reason` other than `false positive` or a `dismissed_by.login` not in `approvers.yaml` (T-528). For each such alert it creates or updates the issue `CodeQL dismissal outside policy: alert <number>`, and a `docchat-security` member reopens the alert or confirms the dismissal by repeating it. A `false positive` dismissal is a determination that the alert isn't a vulnerability: a real high or critical finding has no dismissal path.

## 10. Coverage and gating in CI

### 10.1 Workflows and jobs

**`ci.yaml`** triggers on `pull_request` to `main`; `permissions: {}` at workflow level, each job granting only what it needs (`contents: read`; `id-token: write` only in `preflight-whatif`; `security-events: write` only for SARIF uploads; `deployments: read` only in `migrations`); concurrency group `ci-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: true`; runner `ubuntu-24.04`; every action pinned to a full commit SHA; `actions/checkout` with `persist-credentials: false`. Every job is a required check of the main-branch ruleset (`01-repository.md` section 2.6).

| # | Job | Timeout | Steps |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `lint` | 20 min | setup-uv; setup-node from `.node-version`; `uv sync --locked --all-packages --all-groups`; `npm ci` in `apps/web` and `tests/e2e`; `pre-commit run --all-files --show-diff-on-failure`; `check_forbidden_files.py` (T-516); `check_outbound_clients.py` (T-514); `check_mypy_untyped.py` (T-517); `uv run lint-imports` (T-513); `check_test_layout.py` (T-515); `check_k6_lock.py` (T-580); k6 from the lock, `k6 inspect` on each scenario and the `lib.js` self-test (T-581); the step as a whole is T-512 |
| 2 | `python-tests` | 45 min | `docker compose up -d --wait` with a generated SQL password; `uv run pytest --cov=docchat_core --cov=docchat_api --cov=docchat_ingestion --cov=docchat_ops --cov=docchat_model_stub --cov-branch --junitxml=reports/pytest.xml`; per package `uv run coverage report --include=<package source path>/* --fail-under=<threshold>` (T-519); `docker compose down -v` with `if: always()`; the job as a whole is T-520 |
| 3 | `web` | 20 min | `10-web-client.md` section 1.11: `npm ci`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run format:check`, `npm test -- --coverage`, `npm run build` with `check-dist`, the `generate:api` drift check; uploads `apps/web/dist` for `e2e` |
| 4 | `contract` | 10 min | `01-repository.md` section 7.4 (T-042) |
| 5 | `migrations` | 15 min | section 10.3 (T-532) |
| 6 | `security` | 20 min | pip-audit, npm audit, zizmor, deptry and gitleaks write their reports to `build/audit/`; `uv run python scripts/ci/security_gate.py` (section 9) |
| 7 | `images` | 45 min | Buildx builds of `docchat/api`, `docchat/ingestion` and `docchat/model-stub` without push; their smoke steps (`01-repository.md` section 4); Trivy and the gate (T-523, T-525) |
| 8 | `e2e` | 30 min | Playwright `specs/web/` on the projects `chromium`, `firefox`, `webkit` and `msedge` against the shipped build of job 3 (section 2.13; `10-web-client.md` section 13.2), `retries: 1`, `forbidOnly: true`, trace kept on failure |
| 9 | `infra-validate`, `preflight-whatif` | 30 min each | `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.5; what they must pass is section 8.1 |
| 10 | `infra-tests` | 20 min | section 8.1 |

**`codeql.yaml`**: CodeQL with `security-extended` for `python`, `javascript-typescript` and `actions`, on pull requests to `main`, pushes to `main` and Mondays at 03:00 UTC; `permissions {contents: read, security-events: write}`; no Azure sign-in.

**`nightly.yaml`**: daily at 02:30 UTC.

| Job | Environment and identity | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `test-credential-expiry` | `dev-tests`, `id-docchat-citest` on the test runner (`runs-on: ${{ vars.DOCCHAT_CI_PRIVATE_NETWORKING == 'true' && fromJSON('{"group":"docchat-ci-private"}') \|\| 'ubuntu-24.04' }}`); by default `firewall.py open --target vault --env dev` before the check and `close` after it, and `close` again under `if: always()` | T-545 |
| `deployed-full` | calls `deployed-tests.yaml` with `environment: dev`, `suite: full` | section 6.5 |
| `deployed-infra` | Sundays only; calls `deployed-tests.yaml` with `environment: dev`, `suite: infra` | section 8.2 |
| `gitleaks` | none | gitleaks over the full history (T-526) |
| `trivy-rescan` | `dev`, the `dev` deploy identity, concurrency group `docchat-registry` | rescans the digests deployed to `dev` (the `release-manifest` artifact of the newest `deploy.yaml` run whose `deploy-dev` job succeeded, read with `actions: read`) and to `prod` (the images tagged with the commit of the newest `deploy-prod-*` tag, resolved under AcrPush); it reads no site; a missing or expired artifact fails the job; a finding opens or updates an issue (`83-infrastructure.md` sections 6.8 and 10.15) |
| `model-watch` | `dev`, the `dev` deploy identity | T-435 |
| `codeql-dismissals` | none; `permissions {contents: read, security-events: read, issues: write}` | section 9.4, and the waiver-expiry and exception-review warnings of section 9.3 |
| `load-guard` | `load`, the `load` deploy identity, concurrency group `docchat-env-load` | `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.14 |

**`deployed-tests.yaml`** is section 6.3's and **`load-test.yaml`** section 7.4's. Every job of these workflows that uses the Azure CLI runs `az config set extension.use_dynamic_install=no` right after signing in, and every KQL query goes through the Log Analytics query API (`docchat_testing.logquery`). The workflow rules all workflows follow are `83-infrastructure.md` section 10.2's and are checked by T-456.

### 10.2 The test-layout check

`scripts/ci/check_test_layout.py` (job `lint`) fails CI when a module `src/<package>/<path>/<name>.py` other than `__init__.py`, `__main__.py` and `py.typed` has no `tests/unit/<path>/test_<name>.py` in the same member, unless `[tool.docchat.test-layout] exempt` lists it with a reason (`01-repository.md` section 5.5; T-515). The layout check proves that a test exists for every module; coverage (section 10.4) proves its depth.

### 10.3 Migration checks

Revisions live in `db/migrations/versions/NNNN_<slug>.py` with `revision = "NNNN"` (4 digits, sequential) and a linear `down_revision` (`80-data-model.md` section 2.24). The `migrations` job (checkout with `fetch-depth: 0`, the SQL Server container, `permissions {contents: read, deployments: read}`) asserts exactly one head, runs `upgrade head` on an empty database, runs `alembic check` (no difference between the `docchat_core.sql` metadata and the migrated schema) and runs `scripts/ci/check_migrations.py`.

**Expand, deploy, contract.** `check_migrations.py` parses each new revision and fails when a destructive operation (`op.drop_table`, `op.drop_column`, `op.drop_constraint`, `op.drop_index` of an index created before the revision's parent, `op.alter_column` changing `type_` or setting `nullable=False`, a rename, or an `op.execute` containing `DROP`, `TRUNCATE` or a `DELETE` without `WHERE`) appears in a revision whose docstring doesn't start with `Contract: completes NNNN`, or when that expand revision isn't present in the commit of the latest successful `prod` deployment, read from `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/deployments?environment=prod&per_page=10` and its statuses and checked with `git cat-file -e <sha>:db/migrations/versions/<NNNN>_<slug>.py` (T-074). `downgrade()` raises `NotImplementedError("roll forward")` (T-073): a rollback redeploys earlier images against the current schema, which the rule keeps compatible. App settings follow the same order: removing or renaming one, or setting one outside the range the previous release accepts, ships in a later release than the code that stops needing it, because an images-only rollback runs earlier code against the current settings; code review checks it (`01-repository.md` section 6.1).

### 10.4 Coverage

The Python thresholds are `01-repository.md` section 5.5's, line and branch combined: `docchat_core` 90%, `docchat_api` 85%, `docchat_ingestion` 85%, `docchat_ops` 80%, `docchat_model_stub` 80%, checked per package by the `python-tests` job. The web thresholds are `10-web-client.md` section 1.11's: lines, statements and functions 85%, branches 80%, excluding `src/api/generated/` and `src/main.tsx`, and at least 95% of branches in the modules listed there, checked by the `web` job. `docchat_testing` itself is held by its own unit tests and the meta-tests of section 5.13, not by a threshold. A job below a threshold fails, naming the package or metric (T-519).

### 10.5 What blocks a merge and a release

- **A merge** needs every `ci.yaml` job and `codeql.yaml`'s analysis green, with no open CodeQL alert of security severity high or critical. A failed test is never re-run into a pass by CI: pytest has no rerun plugin, Vitest has no retry, and Playwright's single CI retry reports a test that passed on retry as flaky in the job summary, where the next pull request that touches its spec fixes it. No test is skipped to merge: `xfail_strict = true` makes an unexpected pass fail, and a skip needs a reason that names a condition outside the code (an environment without a capability, such as the `norole` case of section 6.1).
- **A `prod` deploy** needs the `dev` `smoke` suite green (`deploy.yaml` job `test-dev`) and, for the changes section 7.9 lists, the load verdicts attached to the approval; `prod_smoke` failing fails the workflow and notifies the approvers (runbook "Rollback").
- **A stage of `90-build-order.md`** is accepted when every test its acceptance record names is green, the stage-acceptance and rehearsal rows of section 5 included.

### 10.6 Required test kinds and their proofs

| Kind | Proven by |
|---|---|
| Unit tests for every module, including each method's chunker on the four fixture documents | T-515, T-519, T-182, T-183 |
| Authorisation matrix over every route, `GET /healthz` the only public one | T-001, T-565 |
| Cross-project and cross-user isolation on every data path | T-002, T-003 |
| Streaming: framing, order, heartbeats, disconnects, drain | T-263, T-265, T-267, T-268, T-272, T-273, T-597 |
| Event-loop safety | the loop guard (section 2.8; T-561), T-245, T-196, T-591 |
| Idempotency of ingestion | the nine tests T-166 to T-174, T-175, T-587 |
| Data access: single-partition Cosmos DB, index-backed SQL | T-301, T-067, T-595 |
| Telemetry holds no content | T-489, T-488, T-486 |
| Load: the scaling targets | section 7.8 |
| Infrastructure: build, lint, policy, what-if | section 8.1 |
| Security scans at their thresholds | section 9 |

## Acceptance criteria

1. Every test, check and verdict that a numbered acceptance criterion or `SEC-nn` statement of another file names as its proof has exactly one row in section 5; every row of a pull-request level has a test whose name carries its identifier, and no test name carries an identifier outside the catalogue. Proof: the catalogue check (T-569).
2. `docker compose up -d --wait` brings up SQL Server, Azurite, the Cosmos DB emulator and the model stub from their pinned digests on `127.0.0.1` only, and an integration session with any of them down fails rather than skips. Proof: the compose service meta-test (T-563) and the `python-tests` job (T-520).
3. With `DOCCHAT_LOCAL_EMULATORS=true` on an Azure host, or an `http:` endpoint without the flag, each app exits with code 78 naming `local_emulators_in_azure` or `insecure_endpoint`. Proof: the start-up refusal unit tests (T-117).
4. The model stub serves the `openai` 3.31.0 client's streamed, structured and embedding calls, honours every directive and qualifier of section 2.3, refuses unsupported parameters, screens as Prompt Shields does with its 5-document and 10,000-character limits, throttles every Nth request and records requests. Proof: the stub conformance, directive, screening, throttling and parameter tests (T-573, T-574, T-575, T-576, T-577).
5. The loop guard fails a test whose coroutine blocks the loop for 150 ms or leaves a pending task destroyed, passes 50 ms and an awaited sleep, and refuses `allow_blocking` outside `tests/support/tests`. Proof: the loop guard meta-test (T-561).
6. Every integration session fails when an exported span, log record, event or metric data point holds a canary, an email-shaped or a JWT-shaped value, and names the item. Proof: the canary audit meta-test (T-562) and the session-wide canary audit (T-489).
7. `CosmosAudit` fails a chat-path call without its full partition key and a `[userId]` prefix query other than `listSessions` and the after-response extraction re-check; the plan audit fails a request-path statement that scans a table above 1,000 rows. Proof: the Cosmos DB audit and plan audit meta-tests (T-566, T-567), and the audits themselves (T-301, T-067).
8. The authorisation matrix covers every operation of the exported OpenAPI document with its three access extensions, has `GET /healthz` as the only public route, and its generator fails an app with a route missing its extension, differing not-found bodies or fewer than three cases. Proof: the authorisation matrix test (T-001) and the matrix generator meta-test (T-565).
9. Every data path of section 4.2 has an isolation entry with a negative case, every entry passes with `CosmosAudit` confirming each audited partition key is the caller's, and a path without a negative entry fails the suite. Proof: the isolation suite (T-002) and its registry completeness check (T-003).
10. Each fake (`FakeSearchGateway`, `FakePromptShields`, `FakeLayoutAnalyzer`) passes the same contract cases as the client it replaces over recorded responses, and `OrchestrationHarness` replays orchestrators identically after a simulated crash. Proof: the fake contract suites (T-572) and the orchestration harness unit test (T-568).
11. By default a token-holding job on a standard runner reads each alias's password as `id-docchat-citest` only inside its read window, is refused outside it, and leaves no address outside the shared allowlist on the vault; under the private-networking option a token-holding job on a runner outside `docchat-ci-private` fails its token step with `test_vault_unreachable` before any test runs, and on the runner group reads each alias's password through the private endpoint. Proof: the deployed test-vault network test (T-459) and the firewall script unit tests (T-476).
12. Every alias of section 6.1 yields a token whose `aud`, `scp` and `roles` match its row, and the deployed access checks give `outsider` `404 project_not_found`, `norole` `403 role_not_assigned` (or a skip carrying `DOCCHAT_TEST_NOROLE_SKIP_REASON`), `member` `403 admin_required` and `admin` `isAdmin: true`. Proof: the deployed access checks (T-026) and the deployed no-role test (T-027).
13. `DeployedApiClient` and `lib.js` retry exactly once after `retryAfterSeconds` on `429 too_many_requests` and `429 write_rate_limited`, and pass every other `429` to the test. Proof: the deployed client unit test (T-582) and the k6 library self-test (T-581).
14. In `dev`, the crash-resume test ends with both runs `completed`, no activity completed before the restart running a second time, and at least one activity starting after it. Proof: the deployed crash-resume test (T-587) and the resume check unit test (T-585).
15. Every job of `deployed-tests.yaml`, `nightly.yaml` and `load-test.yaml` signs in as the one identity sections 6.3, 7.4 and 10.1 give it; every job that reads `DOCCHAT_CI_TEST_CLIENT_ID` runs in `dev-tests` or `load-tests` on the test runner expression of section 6.3 and ends with the always-run `firewall.py close --target vault` step; no job holding `id-token: write` runs `npm` or a Docker build. Proof: the workflow-rules infrastructure check (T-456).
16. Test passwords are rotated with a 30-day expiry, and the nightly check warns 7 days before any expiry and fails on an expired one. Proof: the `testusers rotate` unit tests (T-544) and the test-credential expiry check (T-545).
17. The load generator always exits 0 and reports k6's result only through `K6_EXIT` or `K6_BOOTSTRAP_FAILED`; the verdict fails a log without either; `s3-rate` picks the smallest bound; `C-db` and `C-cap` fail on their conditions. Proof: the generator bootstrap, `s3-rate` and verdict unit tests (T-579, T-583, T-584) and the k6 lock check (T-580).
18. At the acceptance of the load-test stage, the verdicts of S1 (S1-a to S1-k), S2 (S2-a to S2-e), S3 (S3-a to S3-e, or an `inconclusive` run followed by the re-run section 7.5 prescribes), S4 (S4-a to S4-c) and S5 pass with `C-db` and `C-cap`, and the alert probe passes P-A41 and P-A42. Proof: the load scenarios and the alert probe (T-591, T-592, T-593, T-594, T-595, T-596).
19. Every pull request passes Bicep build, lint and `build-params` for the three environments, `check_bicep_modules.py`, PSRule without an unsuppressed failure, what-if, and the compiled-template tests of section 8.1, and each negative environment-file fixture fails the build. Proof: the `infra-validate` build checks, the PSRule runs, the pull-request what-if and the `infra-tests` job (T-421, T-423, T-431, T-125).
20. The deployed infrastructure suite passes on `dev` every week and after every change under `infra/` reaches it. Proof: the tests of section 8.2, among them the key-rejection, policy-denial, network and diagnostic-settings coverage tests (T-441, T-443, T-444, T-449).
21. Any high or critical finding of any scanner of section 9.2 fails the build whatever `waivers.yaml` holds, except Trivy's DS002 on the ingestion image and an image's HIGH or CRITICAL OS-package vulnerabilities without a fix while their exceptions of section 9.3 are valid, and an exception past its review date or naming anything else fails the build; a lower finding passes only with a valid, unexpired waiver of at most 90 days approved by a listed approver; a pip-audit finding without a rating fails like `HIGH`; a high or critical CodeQL dismissal with another reason than `false positive`, or by an unlisted handle, fails the nightly run. Proof: the security gate unit tests (T-527) and the CodeQL dismissal check unit test (T-528).
22. A package below its coverage threshold, a module without its mirrored unit test, or a destructive migration outside a contract revision whose expand revision has reached `prod`, fails its pull request naming the package, module or revision. Proof: the coverage gate (T-519), the test layout check (T-515) and the migration-check script unit tests (T-074).
